He does not press, which is worse than pressing. **Irreversible.** Relief curdled: a man who reads paper is slower than a man who reads bodies and never has to hurry. |
| 123 | One True Sentence | Day 14, wash house, lamp lit, water running. The girl of twenty-four has fourteen days of list and one question she could not answer: a carrier asked whether the licensed one at the stone end is licensed by Qinghe or by the county, and she said she did not know, because she copies lists and does not read them. Ning Yan says the sentence out loud once — by the county — and is not thanked and does not thank her. Three washerwomen heard it and by the end of the week it is in a market, for free. The county's written request for the furnace schedule goes up the quarter's return sheet on the girl's own hour, entered by her, in the crate on the twenty-eighth. She is alive. |
| 124 | The Second Application | Day 17, county office and the tower. What relicensing is: the same three legs, an hours record continuous from the day the tithe book opened, a new ceiling in her own hand. **Condition two is the door**: hire hours are not furnace hours and no amendment can put them there — **but the annex is the way round, hours certified onto the schedule are furnace hours by certification and not by place, legal since the clause was written, and nobody has ever paid for it.** The annex puts her hours in the quota book and unlevels the number that has been level since the spring. The claim rides on the new stamp, last, required. She asks nobody for anything and writes it up in wash code. |
| 125 | The Dead Writ | Day 20, roofless Hall. She asks for the seal and gets nine and not Foundation. **Lu Kang says the only thing he has said since the spring** — a member at nine is inside their own schedule and examinable there by any office with a stamp and a reason — and says *tell me why not* and *there is no version of nine tonight I will put three marks on*. **They are not refusing.** The writ is dead and not revoked, in one clause. The leg order inverts in front of her: they will not seal a sheet saying nine while her hours are in two places at once. She holds the second book in her coat and does not take it out, because she has nowhere to put it. |
| 126 | The Certified Copy | Day 23, the county hour. **BATCH MIDPOINT.** Eight feet from the counter, a man puts coin on open wood for a certified copy of the Qinghe furnace schedule, keeps the receipt in his own hand, folds it square against the edge, and reads the whole of it at the public counter. He stops on one, two, three, the gap, five, and says two true things to a young clerk in the voice of a man making an observation: **the next stage must be announced or the paper fails its own terms**, and **a licensee capped at five cannot be examined past five**. The clerk agrees with him out loud. Sun He hears both and says *he is right about the stage column* without stopping writing. She does nothing, because the only move is to lie to a clerk in front of a queue. The dread is of being **correct**. |
| 127 | Under a Misspelling | Day 25. A strip of sacking on the door of a lodging house above the tanner's, in the hand of a woman who cannot read: *Chou Zhyan*. The doubled consonant is a clerk's mistake made from memory; she has watched the Hall copyist make the other one for a year. **She does not go up the stairs**, because he has no deadline and she would be giving him a reason to be in Lowwater. He is on Feng's fourth stair for the fifth time and has never once asked for anything. Sun He wrote it up on the twenty-fourth and is required to tell the maintaining members. Fear that is entirely administrative: no ropes, no ditch, no counting room. |
| 128 | The Quiet Book | Day 27, the corridor at the west end. They will seal. The seal is bought, at a price, and the price is the second book. **She states it in the room — if I use it, the seal is bought, and a bought signature is a purchase and not a sponsorship — and they say *then don't*, and she doesn't.** Nothing about the quiet book is lodged, copied or spoken anywhere in ch 119–178. Lu Kang says out loud why he is signing, for the record, because in a year one of the three will be asked. **Three men trust her a little more afterwards, which is the most frightening thing that has happened to her this volume.** What she cannot get back is the not-knowing. Irreversible decision, paid in silence. |
| 129 | The Order of the Crate | Day 28 — the quarter. The Hall clerk writes her name in the wrong order on the certification and Lu Kang reads it and does not correct it. The county reads a crate in the order it is stacked; she works that out, does not tell a sixty-one-year-old keeper how to do his job, and asks for the schedule on top and a list in her own hand on the very top of that. **Feng coughs on the stair and does not offer to carry anything.** She gets the crate down four flights and down four miles of road and will not put it on a cart, and carries it up the county's own stair to the fourth floor, where a boy of seventeen runs one finger down four months of her column and finds nothing. The whole relicensing rests on a true record. No bracket. |
| 130 | Level With the Asked | Day 31, the quota office at the back of the furnace building at Qinghe. Four tables, one window, four benches in the anteroom, two hours and eleven minutes of waiting. The asked figure is a hundred and six and she can work thirty-four, and he will not weight it for one arm and will not close a season early, and he makes **her write the figure she can stand behind in her own hand: fifty-eight.** He asks his one question in a year — *yours alone, or the crew's* — and she says hers alone, and it is the right question, about the annex and not about her. He signs the second clearance in one stroke without looking up and says the early report of ch 34 out loud under the desk, unasked, and is not forgiven and is not thanked. Something close to respect that she does not convert into anything. |
| 131 | Nine in Her Own Hand | Day 35, the roofless Hall. Pei An reads it standing, Deng Chao reads it out, Lu Kang asks four questions and none of them is about her intentions — and then says, in front of two men and a clerk, that this is the hardest signature of his life and he is putting it down anyway, because the alternative is a member handed over with her schedule and **this Hall is a book that has been opened**. Three marks and a stamp. **She writes her ceiling for the third time in the book: nine, in her own hand** — and re-enters her own defect with a new sentence under it: *the defect restores nothing*. The second book is not mentioned by anybody in the corridor. The register still reads *reconciled*. She thanks none of them and wins and pays for it in the same hour. |
| 132 | Relicensed | Day 39, the county office on the square, ninth in the queue, with the quarter's return already on Sun He's table. He stands up and reads the application **in order, out loud, to a room**, and reads seven conditions: the second amended so annexed hours are furnace hours by certification and not another schedule, the sixth new one that the annex returns with the quarter on the same crate in the same order, and **the claim the seventh and the last thing on the paper**, entered because he is required to. The stamp falls. He does not say *you may pick up your licence* this time and she notices. The Hunter is eight feet back at the counter filling in a form; she does not look at him. **Channel: about three weeks.** She does not tell him to stop. |

### Batch 0002 (ch 133–148) — audit and mercy. WRITTEN, 36,742 words over sixteen chapters.

| Ch | Title | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 133 | The Two Clocks | Day 42. The double schedule runs: furnace floor at Qinghe from the ninth hour, the lane behind a well at Lowwater, four miles of road between them on cleared days, county hour at the third hour. **Four loads and eight coppers gone every county hour** — a quarter of a day's pay for the privilege of standing in a room. **The ceiling at nine feels like the roof on a building she already lived in.** The problem is mechanical: the Hall's copyist has been certifying the annex by converting Da Shan's **loads** at the Hall's two-armed rate, so the column reads thirty-one hours against fifty-eight asked. She pays an hour of the furnace day every day to carry a slate frame four miles for a stamp, and the Hall clerk writes **irregular** on the front of the Qinghe furnace schedule and stamps it at the second hour. Gao of the crew reads it on the floor and asks one question; she answers it in one sentence and goes back to the cold end. **No bracket.** |
| 134 | The Annex Is Work | Day 48. What the relicensing bought, on a slate in three columns: a ceiling is a permission and not a body; hours in a column are work and not a wage; the claim travelling on the licence is protection and is exposure. What it cost: an hour a day, four loads a county hour, four miles twice. **And the sentence she had walked past for four months** — a member licensed to practise past registration **may be taught past her stage**, under countersignature, at that county. She does the arithmetic of a pupil in eleven minutes: a master who taught a servant-born with the eight hundred jin against her would enter it in his own hand and send it to a county that reads it in order, and not one of the three men at the west end would put his name to it twice. **She decides to build the stage herself, out of load, on the work** — the only thing she is paid for. The first licence is still crooked over her bunk going soft. **No bracket.** |
| 135 | Six | Days 48 to 81. Six weeks of the double schedule, 240 loads, forty-one stamps, four loads a county hour. **The girl of twenty-four brings a copied carriers' slate with twelve household lines on the back of it**, from a Lowwater counting room whose man counted her slate with his thumb; she is not thanked and had told three washerwomen in advance so Ning Yan could not be. A dressed block rolls at the stone end and her right hand does nothing for four seconds in front of forty people. On the eighty-first day at the water channel at the third hour the stage comes up out of the load, and she orders the announcement at her own order at the fourth hour. **Bracket — the layer's first line in twenty-one chapters, carrying the ceiling of nine.** The cost, on the page: the announcement says **at Lowwater**, and there is one building in that town where a sentence can be turned into a door, and a counting room has opened its book again. **Nine counties, told on purpose.** |
| 136 | The Lawful Request | Day 83. He comes up the last flight for the seventh time, knocks, and leaves a folded sheet on the top stair: a lawful request for the paper the stair carries upward for the quarter, stamped, with six words in a margin in a hand she has not seen — **including what is not entered.** She works out in four seconds that she could go to the county and stop it and says **no** out loud to nothing, because a request carries a reason and goes in a book. A keeper cannot refuse, delay, or answer partly. He buys nothing and is eight feet from a desk that is empty. She takes a **guard's hire on the Lowwater road** at twenty coppers a day and a quarter of the train, five weeks to the close of the season's road. **No bracket.** |
| 137 | A Keeper Answers | Day 86. Feng writes the answer in one sitting, evenly, in one fair hand, and she sits on the stool and reads it. Five sheets: the working lines, four hundred and eleven volumes by shelf and number, **the column at five for twenty-two years**, the list of the four county copies walled behind the fifth item — one of them struck — and, as item five, **the fair line she wrote about herself at the back of a fair volume off a warm shelf**. The intake line at the ninth hour is on the form and is **not examined as a change** and she lets it go because he is right that a request for the stair is not a request for a line. **Irreversible: she does not ask him to leave it out.** He tells her he should have done it and he is glad she did not and both of those are true. **No bracket.** |
| 138 | The Four Behind the Fifth | Day 89. It takes **a boy of seventeen with a finger and about forty minutes**. A quarter's bundle comes up and a clerk of four years writes on the return: returned five sheets against an expected six, missing item the entry of the four copies. Nobody has complained and nobody has done a wrong thing. At the counter she asks one question — does it go up — and is told that is not a thing anybody asks. **The concealment was hers in effect:** she put four sheets in a dead man's coat in the thaw. The first desk in the square is **empty for the first time since the snow**, and the fear is that the calendar has moved. **No bracket.** |
| 139 | A Name Nobody Keeps | Day 90. The county finds the stroke on copy three of four, undated, unattributed. Three clerks and a ledger from the high shelf produce **four hundred and sixty entries and one hundred and nine from outside the bound, every one of them from an office a county can put on a line.** Sun He writes the line himself: *on a request from an office not entered in this county's request register in eleven years.* The tower is emptied: four sheets go into a satchel and are read at the county's table. Feng is asked about dates, not about a hand. **The suspension: one quarter off the stair, a clerk carries the upward bundle, the volumes, the stipend, the cough, the house and the column at five all stay**, and *the district does not terminate an examination.* Yuan says one sentence about the office and will not build a room out of it. **No bracket.** |
| 140 | Paid in Sequence | Day 92. The road below the fourth bridge, dry for the first time in eleven days. A bounty man in boots and a good coat is reading a boy of fourteen his own description, out loud, in the flat voice of a man doing his work. **She sends the boy up the grade.** Yuan tells her first: out of her foundation, does not return, **it takes the order of the road — the pace she spent five weeks building — and it buys about ten weeks out of about three**, and he cannot know whether what she builds instead will arrive. She says yes. **She kills him with the staff in her left hand, one arm, no order left, on her shoulders reading.** The cold opens and she loses two seconds and she very nearly dies. **Fresh bracket.** He gets to the third cart. **Channel: about three weeks → about ten weeks.** The tax takes the road pace and cannot give it back, and the boy walks to Lowwater. She tells the Watch the whole of it flat and does not soften it. **A second body, and the eight hundred jin standing exactly where it stood.** |
| 141 | Oil for the Cough | Day 95. He knocks on the frame of the door for the first time and shuts it behind him. A Listener's appointment: a district wage quarterly, oil and a hundredweight of coal to the door in the first week of the cold, a chair in the county office on reading days at the second hour with the county's fire — **in exchange for reporting the stair upward truly and in full, whether or not it is entered.** The fourth line reads that it may be set aside on request from without the bound, on the same terms as a designation. **The hook is not hidden, and he says so twice.** The third of his three things is that he did not choose the keeper, and that there is a member of this county in the sixth stage whose hours are on two of the five sheets, and he has not mentioned her. She stands in the room and says nothing, because saying it would oblige a man of sixty-two to answer it. **Feng asks for one more night and sends him away with the answer due at the ninth hour tomorrow. No bracket.** |
| 142 | A Tower He Cannot Feed | Day 96. She spends the whole day looking for one honest reason to tell him not to take it. The suspension has left him a tower he cannot feed paper through — the clerk takes the quarter's bundle down four flights in a satchel — and the lamp has started burning an hour past the copying. **The coal comes up the Lowwater road in about eleven days and the road shuts with the weather and not with him**, and a hundredweight is about four weeks out of twelve, so there is no argument: he has already worked eleven winters like this one and come out of them. The girl of twenty-four tells her, unasked, that a man with a chair and a fire is a man who does not want anything off anybody, **because everything he has then comes up a stair without him asking.** At the ninth hour of the next day she comes up four flights and he is at the bench with the sheet and he has not picked up the brush. **No bracket.** |
| 143 | Mercy With a Hook | Day 97. **The batch's irreversible step, and it is Feng's.** He signs the third line in a fair hand with her standing at the end of the bench, and he gives her ninety seconds in order — wage, oil and coal, the warm room — and then says the thing that is hers before she can say it: *you are one of the reasons. You are in two of the five sheets. You are not in the four that were missing.* He tells her he will be dead before it goes wrong and she will not. **Relief that he is alive and fed, immediately curdled** into the knowledge that the man who held her hours in a fair hand is now the office's man. She does not thank him and does not forgive him. On the ninety-ninth he brings his Listener's first return down to the lane himself: the bundle's contents with a fresh line at the top, *Appointed this quarter. Reporting in full from the next.* **The Hunter will never have to stand on that stair again.** She does not lose the paper. **No bracket.** |
| 144 | A Hired Guard | Day 99. The contractor puts a second man on the train: **Tan**, paid out of his own pocket, who takes the wheel on the fourth day of the second week — a drag pin gone at a quarter of a mile, eleven tons, eleven inches of clearance, the river four feet below the ditch. **She did not see it.** The road pace she spent at ch 140 was the thing that told her where a wheel was going, and she has been walking this road twice a day with a hole in her. Escort work is inside her scope and still has to be annexed and certified like everything else. She is paid as a licence holder and worked as a woman with one arm and **both of those are on paper and only one of them will ever be read.** She will not lie to the contractor and will not put the reason in a shed foreman's mouth. **No bracket.** |
| 145 | Loaded and Watched | Day 104. **Four hours short of fifty-eight, with about sixteen days of road left in the season.** The gap is the seven weeks she worked and did not write in the frame, because the frame is written at the ninth night hour and the road starts at the ninth hour. **She works the difference rather than writing it**, because a column is a record of a body. What the stage is being built out of is now the *opposite* of what it was at six: not knowing in advance, but being wrong and going back and looking. **Feng has not asked her a question in nine days and she worked out why, and it was not tact.** The fever comes in at the third hour halfway down a grade, takes three days, and she walks four miles with it and tells Tan not to say. **No bracket.** |
| 146 | Under Licence Scope | Day 110. Escort work has to be annexed and it is not the road's problem. The Hall copyist puts her slate on an eleven-deep stack — two days, and the season's road shuts with the weather. **The second hand is a carriers' run list, and the girl of twenty-four has been copying two hundred of them since the thaw and nobody has ever wanted one.** She pays eight coppers a sheet on the day of hand-over and not before, and explains why, and does not thank her and does not say thank you when the girl stops her. A clerk of four years finds *escort* in a district tariff and reads the scope line and the amended second condition and confirms it is inside her scope. **The Hunter is at the first desk, buys nothing, writes for four minutes, and looks at her for a count and a half.** Freedom and exposure came in the same envelope from the same sheet. **No bracket.** |
| 147 | The Last Load | Day 114. The season's road closes on the hundred and seventeenth day and nobody tells anybody. She is on it until the last train: stone, for a weigh-house that will not be finished before the cold. The fourth load will not sit and the contractor wants it anyway; it goes over on the turn above the ditch, Tan takes the horse and she takes the wheel with her left forearm, and it is left at the side of the road with two stones and a marker. **Four and a half hours against it, at the rate. That is the four.** At the second hour of the next day the column is **level — fifty-eight asked and fifty-eight worked** — and the Hall clerk writes *asked and worked level at the close* and not *complete*, because complete is for an ordinary member. She stands in a Hall with no roof and does the sum: the aperture, the defect line, the fourth stage on no paper, the road shut. **Feng is on the fourth stair with a satchel.** **No bracket.** |
| 148 | Three Counts | Day 116 to 130. Two weeks of fever, eleven days in the bed. **Condensing 7, announced at her order**, from the mat at the second hour of the night on the fourth night of the fever, and she asks whether she had to and whether he will tell her in a month it was the wrong thing; he answers that he has told her once and once is the number. **The fever is the cost, and it takes the stair:** on the eleventh day she stops on the second landing for four seconds, and nine days later at the top of the third flight in the crew quarters it is **three counts, and it does not come back to two.** She declines the county carrier in the three words a form allows for. She listens from the loft to a fair hand copying her upward for four days and does not go down. The season's road is shut, the lane has four weeks left in it, the second licence is still in the coat lining and is not nailed up, and the first one is still crooked over her own bunk going soft. **Bracket. No price named, nothing foreshadowed.** |

| 149 | Carried Down the Stair | Day 133. The handover paper has been on the mat of the tower loft for four days, unopened: county-good, folded twice, **not addressed, not sealed, and signed by no person.** She could read a licence or a county hand; she cannot read a man, and that is the whole of the difficulty. She goes down four flights and says it out loud **on the copying floor, in front of Feng** — *I am going to have him read a man through my eyes* — and has him enter it. **It is a tower change, not a request:** a thing that happened in the building, entered in fair hand, going up the quarter. She names the price before the word: **four and a half weeks out of about ten**, told once and slowly. Then she goes up alone and says it. **What it buys:** the room over the tanner's, a board of dates in his hand, the eight remaining proofs in order, a return already sent upward with her hours in it, **and the four sheets of the handover verified true, line by line.** **What it does not buy:** the office name, the eleven, design. He puts it down the same day and **refuses to hold it again — permanently for the volume.** She does not thank him. She does not forgive him. **Ledger taken under a day on her own word. No bracket.** |
| 150 | What Stops Talking | Day 136. **THE MILESTONE. She moves the table out of his room into the county office on the square, because a county writes down everything said in it.** Sun He enters the hearing in the ordinary hand and reads the four sheets at the third hour. The offer: the voice certified as a misread natural echo; the designation drawn down painlessly over one winter; **licence and stages standing**; **the eight hundred jin answered out of the Warden's own purse**, entered as a claim satisfied by a third party, which pays Hou Sanliang in full on a named day. She asks the two questions a competent person asks of a mercy — **who pays, and what happens to the thing in my head** — and gets true answers, including *I do not know what happens to it, and neither does anyone in this room.* And the fourth line of the second sheet, in the ordinary place: **announcements already carried to the bound are not comprised in this instrument and are not affected by it.** True, painless, real, and it leaves four announcements standing with nobody left alive to explain them. **She refuses aloud before two witnesses and signs it in her own left hand.** The clerk brings a Warden's book off a shelf in this county's own room. The paper carries no agent's name; the clerk asks for the agent's name to lay the paper, and **Bai Zhu is named, here, once, by a form.** Sun He writes it into the county book too. **THE VOLUME'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION: she chooses to be hunted rather than handed over, with every term verified true first. She does not thank Yuan for the reading and does not forgive him for the price of it. No bracket.** |
| 151 | The Attendance | Day 139. The district's standing order, entered by Sun He without comment: a member who has declined an instrument laid by an officer of that office attends that office **once a month**, in person, signed, on a fixed day. She is the first this office has had. **The refusal does not touch the licence; it is on her member's sheet**, which is copied at the quarter and at any application, and a member's sheet goes up with whatever else goes up — so it goes to the Hall at Qinghe, on the furnace schedule, under her hours, **to a crew of nine who read their own schedule.** A missed attendance is entered separately from her hours and cannot be paid for in hours. She does the season against it on the third flight: **the dread stops being about a man with a calendar and becomes about arithmetic.** **No bracket.** |
| 152 | Slate and Chalk | Day 140. The board behind the well is wiped and not chalked and there is nothing on it. Two days a week, then one. **There is a quarry three miles east on the Verge road — eleven men, a hoist worked by four, eighteen coppers a load, the same stone as the fourth table, which is why she trusts it.** She looks at it four times before she takes it, and takes it on this day: **day one of the five weeks.** She tells the crew on the floor at the ninth hour and Gao asks *under your licence* and she says *annexed to it, the copyist certifies it, it goes up with the quarter.* She does not tell them about the refusal and works out in eleven seconds **that she would rather say it herself than have a clerk's hand do the first telling in nine counties.** She wants a wage for the first time in four months. **No bracket.** |
| 153 | The Monthly Reading | Day 147. First attendance. **A monthly attendance is read, not merely signed**, because a sheet is copied at the quarter and at any application and a member's sheet is one of those. Sun He reads the stage column aloud to a queue that wants to be served: **one, two, three, five, six, seven** — then the width of two ruled lines. Then the registrar's ruling, **to the room and not to her**, for the second time, and she understands four seconds late that he is not defending her, he is **entering** the ruling. The Hunter attends at the back, writes after, and asks nothing. On the step he says three things: **he will be at the next one; he will never ask her a question; and he could not identify her by face, because the only way she knows his name is a form.** She stands in a lane with her own sheet in her right hand and sees that a county reads a stage column the way it reads a number, and that the only room in nine counties where her life is dramatic is one with three people in it. **No bracket.** |
| 154 | The Signing Hand | Day 150. Ice across the grade where the water runs off the cut. The sledge with no wheel on the near side comes down three loads; the third is behind the hoist and she goes for the handle and takes the weight of the near side to lift it off a man on his knees on the ice, and **the heel of her left hand goes** — the part that turns. She does not stop, does not take a day, does not tell the foreman. **Eighteen coppers a load goes to twelve and does not go back.** The escort-road pace that would have told her a quarter of a mile out was spent at the fourth bridge in the summer and is gone; her left hand was on the bar all morning and she had nothing to say between the two. She goes at it that night with a slate and writes the column **with the flat of the finger** — no wrist turn, every figure finished by a drag out to the right — slower, legible, hers. The ugly luck of it: a county never wanted her hand to be beautiful, only to be hers. **No bracket.** |
| 155 | A Date in a Counting Room | Day 153. The girl of twenty-four brings a slate at the far end of the trough, four minutes early, paid eight coppers on the day. The front is the twelve household lines she already knows. **The back has six words and a date and the two characters that go on a paper when there is money in it** — and the hand is not the hand that writes the front, and it is a better pen. **A counting room does not enter a future date against a claim it is content to wait on.** It is a date to *answer* by, for entry for execution, with everything required already on file — which means a copy of her licence with the claim on the back of it and an address four miles up a mountain. **A date turns a claim into a schedule.** She asks the girl *where did it come from*, and knows as she asks that it puts the girl under the line, and the girl says so, and asks for it to be said aloud between them. **Hou Sanliang does not appear and is not met.** No bracket. |
| 156 | Walking In | Day 156. The carrier's yard, the season's road shut. **The boy got in** — four days a week at a yard, a day washing cloth, owed four coppers and paid three. He gave a stranger a road and a county because the stranger had hold of his ear and asked nicely. He is **fifteen now, not fourteen.** He is in the Watch's book in one line, as a witness to an entered act, and that is the only line in nine counties that says he saw anything. She does not give him money, because money handed to a carrier's boy gets written on the back of something. Instead she turns to the yard clerk with a good hand and says out loud, in front of eleven men, that the description is about her and not about him. **The cost lands in the same beat:** she has given her crew, her county and the road to forty men who cannot read, in a yard a man over a tanner's door can walk into any afternoon. She does not go back. **No bracket.** |
| 157 | Nothing Chalked | Day 158. **The four weeks are up.** Da Shan wipes the frame and does not chalk it and goes and has a smoke; four men at the fourth table and none at the first. **There is no paid work in this county for a licensed woman with one arm until the thaw — except the quarry, which is three miles east, and it runs about six more weeks, and then there is a tin of two hundred and thirty coppers.** She does not tell Da Shan she is leaving, for the same reason she did not tell the crew: telling him would be a thing she owed him. She sleeps two nights a week in the loft and finds **Feng warm for the first time in twenty-two years**, and says the one sentence she has been avoiding out loud instead of letting him work it out: *I do not forgive you, and also you are sixty-two and warm, and I am not going to be the one who spends it.* **No bracket.** |
| 158 | The Weight Ticket | Day 161. The winter return is called for at the Hall, with the annex, irregular twice — **and it goes with her member's sheet**, which means the refusal goes to the Hall too. Then the real problem: **the lane had carriers' run lists entered by the weigh-house and the quarry has none.** It pays by a weight ticket written by the hoist-man at the rope, and weight is not time, and in nine counties there has never been a second hand for one. **A quarry hour is uncertifiable, so the annex does not fill, and a gap in that column is work done and not shown — which is not an absence.** She stands at the rope three days. **Kan** holds it nine seconds and then writes the hour in at the left and the load in at the right, because a ticket with an hour on it is a thing a foreman can be asked about and he is the one holding the pen. Forty-one cards, irregular, stamped. **No bracket.** |
| 159 | Before It Comes | Day 164. **She tells nine men first, on a furnace floor, a little under three weeks before a clerk's hand does it** — and opens with the fact that she refused a piece of paper **twenty-eight days ago**, in front of two witnesses. Gao's whole response is to come and stand on her other side at the grate for four hours and never mention it. On the same night, at the third hour, **the stage arrives**: it goes down about nine hundred feet and stops, and it is hers to hold. Both sides are real. Announce and nine counties have it before breakfast with no date set by anybody, and it is nine — the top of the realm, the number on her licence, the last thing this relicensing will carry her to. Suppress and the county reads nothing having happened, and a man who was right in June reads a lie. She draws the sheet out on the floor of the loft and sees it: **one, two, three, gap, five, six, seven, gap — two gaps, the same width.** “Do not argue with me.” He has said one word in six weeks and says it now and it is the right one. **No bracket.** |
| 160 | The Second Hole | Days 166 to 175. Five weeks of winter-quarry carrying with the stage inside her the whole way; **it is not a weight, it is an address.** The grief she works it with is the boy of fourteen — now fifteen, owed four coppers and paid three, whom she has seen once for nine minutes and cannot go back to, and she works it in loads the way ch 104 worked hers. **On the thirty-third day of the quarry she shuts the door of an empty hut at the third hour and says no out loud, to a stove, in the ordinary voice.** **It goes down in about eleven seconds. Nothing goes out.** **No bracketed line in this chapter at all.** What the filing records is an hour worked, certified, stamped irregular. **A suppressed stage leaves exactly the same absence as any other hole: the same width.** She gains the feeling of putting a heavy stone down, and loses it to a man at the back of a room who was right in June. **Ledger's standing changes hands: it is hers to order and his to hold only on her word.** Channel: **about three weeks.** No bracket. |
| 161 | Where He Is Right | Day 178. Second attendance, a month after the first. The column is read in order and ends in the width of two ruled lines. **Sun He enters the ruling a third time, with the man in the room** — and both sentences are now in the same county's book: the registrar's *an absence is not a breach*, and the counter's *a paper that does not carry a stage has failed one of its own terms.* He has bought one thing off a county and nothing since, and he paid one coin for the argument. **He is not going to ask her about it, and he says so in one breath so she does not have to spend a morning on it.** She does the arithmetic in the loft and understands that she has been furious with a registrar for being right and has never once stopped to consider what being right had been **for**. **No bracket.** |
| 162 | What Da Shan Told Him | Day 181. The winter crate reached the Hall on day 167 and **the schedule comes out of it and goes on the board; the return itself has not come back down** — it is in a county office four miles off, waiting to be read in front of a room she will not be in. **Nine men read the refusal and say nothing at all** — which is not the silence she spent four weeks being afraid of. Then a carrier tells her, without weight, that a stranger stood at the fourth table in the autumn and **read the crew's own sheet off the wall, all the way down**, the way he read the certified copy off a counter. **Da Shan gave it to him. He did not have to; there is a sheet on the wall and a man asks to read it.** She stands at the board — **four men at the fourth table, none of them her, and two of the eight frames out of the wall** — and understands that **the price of being hunted is paid by a man at a board in the cold, and that its mechanism is a word she put on the front of a schedule herself.** She tells **four** of her crew (Gao and three others) to put four loads a week on his lane on their own time, **without his permission**, so it looks like a crew and not a favour. Gao says he will tell her name if the man asks twice, and does, and Da Shan puts the frame back on the wall himself and never asks her a single question. **No bracket.** |
| 163 | Not Held in This County | Day 184. She walks **the week's annexed hours** down the four miles herself and finds on the shelf by the door, among the things that have gone up, **a request with a number and a date, asking for an instrument not held in this county, for use in this county, after the reading of the winter return.** Nothing sent. Nothing filed. **A field man with a room over a tanner's door cannot unmake her inside a bound; the paper that could is not in this county and not in any county, and the only lawful thing he can do about it is write a line on a sheet and let a clerk give it a number.** Eleven weeks of hunting, and he was doing administration. She does four pieces of arithmetic on the shelf and does none of them. **Ledger is hers to order and his to hold only on her word, and he tells her plainly that the standing being hers is the one good thing that has come out of the run and the reason nothing is going to happen is him.** Eleven weeks of winter against about three weeks of channel — three weeks being what is left of the five and a half she paid four and a half out of ten for, after thirty-six days of rationed use. The second licence is still in oilcloth and is not going to be nailed up. **She does not forgive him.** **No bracket.** |
| 164 | The Last Card | Day 187. **The only paid work in the county runs out.** Bo has put eleven men down to five and has not said why; the face has four days of stone in it that a bar can reach and then nothing until the thaw. He asks her the one question he asks in the whole run — *will you be here in the spring when the face opens* — and she gives him a straight answer. **Kan has written forty-one cards**, one a day the rope runs, hour at the left and load in at the right, and she forecasts forty-three. She takes the four days, and she makes the last of them go up as one line with the county's irregular stamp on the front for the fourth time this year, because she is not going to have days of her hours disappear into a gap in the worst month. Her right arm goes at the first hour on the fourth day, where nobody saw it, because she has built two months of rhythm around the third of the time. **The tin is about two hundred and thirty coppers.** Four miles of hill, a flight of stair that costs three counts, and about six weeks of quarry left. No bracket. |
| 165 | The Pay-Off | Day 189. **THE PHYSICAL CHAPTER.** The last block comes off the lip at the first hour, her right arm goes in the middle of the second piece, and the piece shifts a foot toward her and keeps going. She goes down flat on the lip and holds on. **Kan gets her off backwards in one movement and nobody is hurt.** Bo pays at the rope in the open, off forty-three cards, **her hour on thirty-four of them: a hundred and seventy loads at twelve coppers, two thousand and forty gross, six hundred and eighty of it gone at the hand, one thousand three hundred and sixty in her hand.** He says all of it in front of four men so the man on the fourth bench finds out from him and not in the spring. **She has never had that much money in one place in her life.** And the face is shut: **there is no stone in this county now for twelve coppers a load.** No bracket. |
| 166 | Nine Coppers a Load | Day 191. She finds out what a winter with no wage is made of. The only stone left is a **bank up the east road at nine coppers a load**, and the yard that sells by the load is four days east with the road shut. **She has been buying the stone, not the loads** — the carrying is four men and their boots, and four of her crew do it on their own time. Da Shan tells her himself, in the ordinary voice, that he has been charging her five and is going to charge nine, and that if she had offered to stop he would have said yes and the table would have been dead. **She keeps buying at nine.** Six weeks of it is two hundred and sixteen coppers, the four coppers a load is ninety-six of that, and ninety-six is the entire price of the decision. She pays the girl a quarter in advance to the thaw so the eighth estate has no question to ask. The tin is about one thousand five hundred and ninety and she does the whole of the winter on the back of a slate. No bracket. |
| 167 | The Cold End | Day 194. She is not posted at the cold end. Nobody has the standing to post her and **nobody has posted her**; she signs for her own shift and walks forty feet past nine men every morning. Ten days and nineteen shifts without one of them coming to that end for any reason — and the crew's silence, which she has been reading as respect, **turns out to be distance.** The fourth week of the cold end is the first week she works it without counting her breath, and the build is close and not close. The ninth is not an address the way the eighth was; it is loud and everywhere and there is no door in that building for it. She does not ask for a man to come to the end, because the asking would be the end of the quiet. **She works it alone into the fifth week.** On the fourth morning she finds on the public shelf a sheet requiring a member to be present at the reading of the winter return. No bracket. |
| 168 | The Notice | Day 196. **A notice from a rule, not from a person**: the winter return is read at the second hour on the nineteenth, and a member whose hours are on it stands in the room while they are read. Under it, **a registrar's line, in the ordinary hand, in the ordinary office: a member may see the sheet before it is read and may ask for an hour not to be read to be entered as read and not as absent.** It is in the room she swore at ch 162 she would not stand in. She does the arithmetic of going — four miles down and four up, a morning in four, a third flight with three counts, a morning of the girl's bad leg — and it comes to four or five coppers' worth of a life. **The cost of not going is a column with three holes in it, read into a room that decides.** She goes. No weekday is named anywhere. No bracket. |
| 169 | The Man Who Stayed | Day 197. **Qin, twenty-two, two winters on the crew, stands at the cold end for a whole shift and does not work.** He is looking at her hands and her back, not her face. There were four things she could have asked him and a fifth under all four that mattered, and she does not ask it, because a man of a crew does not get to explain himself in front of the others. **She does not send him back.** The ninth comes up at the fourth hour of that afternoon like water under a door and she gets through the shift on her feet with nine men within forty feet and no door between her and any of them. It sits in her all night like a stone in a boot. **Holding it or letting it go is no longer a question about paper:** letting it go tells nine counties exactly where she stands fourteen days before a referral goes up the stair; holding it a fourth time costs the only argument she has left, because she would then be doing the thing she does in order to be suspected of doing it. She gets up at the fourth hour and walks down four miles. No bracket. |
| 170 | Another Hand | Day 197, later. **The warm chapter, and the last warm room in the volume.** The tower is warm and the lamp is burning and Feng is sixty-two and has been warm for twenty-two years. A district clerk goes up four flights with the upward bundle and comes down empty, **because Feng asked for the stair to be taken off him to be written as permanent.** He has copied out all four of the county's copies of her stage column in fair hand — one, two, three, then two ruled lines, then five, six, seven, then two ruled lines — for the building, not the county. He cannot read what is in the gaps and has not been able to for nine years, and he has done nothing about it for four weeks, and says that is the correct answer and not much of one. She asks one thing: have tomorrow entered **as a tower change, a thing that happened in the building and not a service rendered to a member**, and have the nine copied as an announcement the member made and not one the tower heard. He also tells her to stand at the mark before the ninth hour, not at it, because the setting comes out at the ninth and nine men working is the same nine men and costs her nothing. **Four miles up in ninety minutes, and the third flight costs four counts.** No bracket. |
| 171 | Nine | Day 198, the ninth hour, the cold end. **THE BATCH'S POWER BEAT AND ITS IRREVERSIBLE STEP.** Condensing 8 → 9, **announced at her own order, in the open, witnessed by nine men.** The bracket line is the only bracketed line in the batch. It goes out of the bound in every voice in nine counties at once and **it takes the space with it** — she is full, top to the soles of her feet, with no gap anywhere in her anywhere, and she has never in her life been that. It is over before the setting comes back out of the mark. **Then she tells them out loud, before they can work it out on their own over a fortnight**: a thing is coming on the nineteenth, a man will send a paper up past this county about what is in her head, he has never lied to her, and there are fourteen days between the two. Gao says the sum out loud later in a hall so nine men have heard it from a mouth. **What it cost, named on the page:** nine counties know exactly where she stands a fortnight before the referral goes up, and **the room that was hers by being empty fills with people and stays filled and will never be hers again.** |
| 172 | Eleven Minutes | Day 203. Five days after the ninth hour the schedule comes down off the Hall board with the nine on it and *irregular* on the front for the fourth time in a year, and **nine lines with three gaps in them**. Nine men have noticed the hand — Gao stopped asking her to hold the slate, Qin has walked four miles down the mountain four times to stand eight feet off in a queue while she writes — and a young clerk offers to write it for her and is refused, because **eleven minutes in her hand is four minutes in a clerk's and then there are two hands on it.** At the ninth hour on the floor, in front of all nine, she says it out loud: the heel of her left hand is gone, a third of every load goes and does not come back, the county column takes eleven minutes where it used to take four. Gao's whole response is *then say so at the frame and I will hold it*. **It costs the last private thing she had about her body, she spends it on purpose, and nobody thanks her because she did not tell them to.** No bracket. |
| 173 | The Ceiling Reached | Day 209, the third hour, the county office on the square. The third attendance. **A county has to enter a stage that was announced**, so Sun He enters the nine with the date and the place and the fact that the member made it, and then reads the column down the page, and stops, and does arithmetic. **The ceiling on her licence is nine and she is at nine and there is nothing above nine on the document.** He says why the sponsors sealed nine and not twelve, and that a second relicensing needs a schedule of hours worked at the ceiling being asked for, **and that this county does not keep a schedule of work done past its boundary — not kept elsewhere, not kept at all.** He presses the binder without asking a question and says he is sorry about a specific thing. The Hunter, eight feet back, buying nothing, writes four words a day about a stage that was announced on the date she announced it. **No channel figure in this chapter.** No bracket. |
| 174 | One, Two, Three | Day 212, the second hour, the county office on the square. **THE BATCH CLIMAX.** The winter return is read in public with a queue out into the square. Sun He reads the column in order — **one, two, three, five, six, seven, nine** — and goes over three gaps without a sound, writes the county's four-word formula down the margin three times, writes *Member present* at the foot, says the ruling aloud **and stops, because he has said it three times already and there is no fourth sentence in this county that would be better than the first three.** Then she signs it herself, **eleven minutes with the flat of a second finger in front of a queue that does not shuffle**, the first figure about fifty seconds and the ninth about twenty, and the room says nothing and the clerk does not look at his watch. Then Bai Zhu certifies in a Warden's hand that the thing in her head is real, is unauthorised, and is carried by a licensee in good standing whose seventh condition travels with her and is not answered by the instrument, and **refers the dismantling upward to the Warden, for want of a Wall writ — the only instrument he does not hold, and there is none in this county.** The referral carries her name, her stages in order and her hours, and carries no agent's name. The request on the public shelf goes live and goes on the crate. **The subject is standing in the room, and it is hers at ch 150 that put her there.** She checks, standing there, that she does not wish she had said yes. No bracket. |
| 175 | Barred | Day 213. **The Foundation petition, filed and barred in one day.** Four lines on a sheet of the Hall's paper, written at a bench in the hall: a relicensing with the ceiling at Foundation the first stage, on a relicensed licence with seven conditions, grounded on an hours record continuous from the origin date and held in this county in full. **She folds the filing copy of her own licence in behind the four lines, front outward**, because the seventh condition travels with the paper and the only version of her that goes up that stair should not be four lines in her own hand — and because a thing nobody has looked at cannot be refused. Sun He takes up the clerk's pen instead of his own, which is the difference between a thing entered and a thing ruled. **Received, numbered, routed, barred — not refused — with the ground of the bar entered as the absence of a schedule and not the standing of the applicant**, and a last line he wrote slower: *the answer to this application is not this office's to write.* **Once entered it cannot be withdrawn by her.** It is dated the day after a referral went up out of this county and both papers ride the same crate. She did not hesitate. No bracket. |
| 176 | What Went Up | Day 215. **Everything that can leave this county leaves it, in one crate, on one morning**: the quarterly schedule with *irregular* on the front for the fourth time, the referral, the request for the instrument, and her petition with a number and a bar on it. Two days later the district answers the request the way a district answers — **received, numbered, referred upward, no instrument held in the district either, and an empty space the width of a line where an answer was meant to go.** Nine men read the schedule in the Hall on the twenty-second and **Gao does the sum out loud at the near end, in a hall, on purpose, so nine men have heard it from a mouth instead of off a wall.** Da Shan tells her a man came in on the twelfth, asked how many loads came down that week, wrote it in a book, and he told him because it is his board. The stone she has been buying is ten loads at nine coppers. **About a hundred and forty coppers in the tin.** She does not forgive him and is not going to on the strength of a good winter. No bracket. |
| 177 | Out of the Oilcloth | Day 217. **Small, warm, domestic, competent.** She takes the second licence out of the oilcloth on the bench in the wash house and lays it flat. The front: **Condensing nine** in her own writing and a new line under the defect saying it restores nothing, entered on a loft mat in the summer. The back: she does not read the seven conditions, because she read them once, and she looks at the last line, **the eight hundred jin, seventh and last**, and does not tear it off, and it was still there when the crate went. The Clay Plate stays against her ribs. The first licence comes down off two nails, going soft at the crease of a defect line she wrote herself, and gets rolled into her coat lining before it is unreadable. Four nails, six coppers. **She practises at the foot of the bunk in the dark — three straight out of five, and the fifth splits the pine — and then in daylight in the yard behind the Hall, where the second hour teaches her the third thing: the hand does not want to be watched, and the stroke is something she has been doing rather than letting the arm finish.** She will do it at the seventh hour, when nine men come in and somebody says something about the fire. No bracket. |
| 178 | Straight | Day 219. **THE FINAL IMAGE, all seven elements, then stop.** First week of the deep cold. The fire goes down twice between the fourth hour and the light. Ten people sleep in twelve bunks. She is in the second bunk and she can see the one light in Lowwater from the stair, and **the thing that goes up that tower's four flights this quarter goes up in a district clerk's arms and comes down in his arms empty, because Feng asked for it in writing in the autumn and was not refused and was not thanked for it.** **The licence comes out of the oilcloth for the second day running.** She has two arms' worth of practice at nailing and one arm. She strikes it, it stops a third of the way in, she strikes it again, **and it goes in straight**, and there is no way on earth of correcting it now and no need to. The second nail goes straight too. Then she tells Yuan the thing is gone up the stair past every office she has ever stood in front of, to an office **whose name he still cannot read**, and she says she was never going to stop him. **She asks how much of it is there, and he tells her the truth: about ten days.** Ten days, and about six weeks of winter still to come in it. A licence with nothing on it above nine. Three holes in nine lines and nine counties that have it in order. A petition barred. About a hundred and forty coppers. **She has the Ledger to order and she does not order it.** And in the last line, with the fire down to a bed of ash and ten people asleep, she does not tell him to stop. |

## Volume 3 closed — audited, then reviewed

**Audited against `outline/volume-03.md` line by line at the volume close. PASS on all thirteen named
items.** No title, chapter order, card beat, bracket or ending moved. The full audit table, the repair list,
the findings recorded and not repaired, and the corrections to inaccurate claims in the state files are in
`state/current.md` under `## Volume 3 close audit — CLOSED`, with the close review's reversal of one of the
audit's rules under **Close review repair** in the same block. **What the close changed on the page, for
Volume 4 to read first:**

- **The footprint of nine counties and four counties is a distinction between what is HEARD and what is
  WRITTEN — not between the claim and the licence.** Volume 1 says it in the characters' own mouths at ch
  46: *the same advancement, **in four counties of nine***, and at ch 55: *going round **nine counties** for
  three days, and Du Hong had read **her four counties' versions***. **Nine counties is what is heard; four
  counties is what is written into the books.** One advancement entered in four counties is entered four
  times and billed four times, which is what happened to her father at ch 55. **The close audit got this
  backwards and the close review reversed it: ch 160, ch 163 and ch 176 are back at four counties for the
  stage column and the paper.** The three *heard*-footprint repairs stand (ch 150, 153, 161). Ch 178's *nine
  counties that had it in order* stands and is the reading. The rule is now in `state/open-threads.md`.
- **Hou Sanliang's counting room is in Lowwater, where she is standing.** Ch 163 and ch 177 had it *nine
  counties away*. Both repaired to match ch 173, and both stand.
- **Ch 176 said about eleven weeks of winter where ch 178, four days later, says about six.** Day 184 plus
  eleven weeks, day 219 plus six weeks, and day 215 plus **seven** all land on the same end of the winter,
  so ch 176 was the wrong one. Repaired, and it stands.
- **"Eleven weeks" is retired.** It stood in fourteen chapters of Volume 3 carrying at least five different
  meanings. The state files had recorded it as standing in four chapters. It is now prohibited in Volume 4,
  and the two figures Volume 4 will need are given as derivations: **the Hunter arrived on day 2, and the
  winter ends at about day 261.**
- **"About four seconds" is a rhythm and not a rule** — thirty-six uses across twenty-six chapters, and the
  state files had recorded it as three. The four seconds and the release rule from ch 53–54 are not
  restated anywhere in Volume 3 and must not be in Volume 4.
- **Du Hong's name is used four times** (ch 119, 125, 138, 160), all as remembered weight inside a live
  scene; Cao Lei and Xu An are never named in Volume 3. The burial line is not reopened. Recorded, not
  repaired, and recorded here so it is not rediscovered as an unknown fault.
- **Ch 131's age is now correct.** It read *"She was twenty."* against `bible/characters.md` — *seventeen
  at the start of Volume 1; eighteen in Volume 3*. **Repaired at the close review to *"She was eighteen."***
  rather than cut, so the beat survives and nothing else in ch 119–178 states her age. **The last item on the
  closed-chapter list is closed.**
- **The name in the wrong order is six uses across five chapters** (120, 129 ×2, 149, 158, 162), not five
  across four — corrected at the close review.
- **The chapter-length record was wrong.** Nothing is under the 1,300 floor; **ch 138 (1,779) and ch 151
  (1,653) sit below the 1,800 guide and nine chapters sit above 2,700**, longest ch 140 at 3,193.
  Volume 3 closed at **135,014 words over sixty chapters**, average ~2,250 — **unchanged by the close
  review, whose three reverts and one age fix were one-word swaps.**

## Volume 4 — The Wall (ch 179–238) — CLOSED AND AUDITED

### Batch 0001 (ch 179–192) — ration and silence. WRITTEN, 28,607 words over fourteen chapters, average ~2,043.

**No stage is taken in this batch and there is not one bracketed layer line in fourteen chapters.** Condensing 9 throughout, day 220 to day 253.

| Ch | Title | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 179 | Answer Pending | Day 220. **Volume 4 opens on the public shelf by the door**: eleven things, now twelve, and the twelfth is a county notice with a seal giving the day the season's road east reopens. The eleventh is the district's slip — four words and an empty space. She reads it three times, one more than last time, and works out that **a ruled space is not an unanswered question; it is the place where an entry gets made, by an office above this county.** He says nothing the whole day and she does not spend one sentence finding out why. She counts the tin. **Discovery: a clerk in this county has sealed a date onto a boundary, and she can see the edge of the thing from inside it.** |
| 180 | The Stone and the Fire | Days 221–222. Thirty-six a week at nine coppers a load against about a hundred and forty in the tin; the arithmetic runs to the end of this month, the notice gives the eighth of the next, and **seven days sit between the last of the stone and the road** — the coldest week in the coldest month. She does not tell nine men, because the only fix nine men have is carrying stone on their own time at hours that are on a schedule already stamped *irregular*. Da Shan says the true thing about his board without being asked. The fire goes down twice between the fourth hour and the light anyway, as it has all winter. **Cost.** |
| 181 | His Own Request | Days 223–224. The copying floor, the only warm room in four miles, and no reason at all for being in it. Feng copies her column fair: **one, two, three, a ruled line, five, six, seven, a ruled line, nine.** **She wanted him to ask what is in the gaps.** He does not, and says why: a keeper who asks has begun to want something, and he has wanted nothing since the autumn. His chair in the county office is the only valuable empty chair in the building. She stays forty minutes, spends one sentence of him on nothing at all, and does not thank him. |
| 182 | Not Herself | Days 225–226. She has the Ledger and a room to decide in. She names five things and **refuses four out loud, one at a time, on a mat**: herself out of the hunt, on the same grounds as the refusal she signed in the spring; the eight hundred jin; the rule his speech runs on; the crew. Four sentences spent and nothing bought. What is left is **a hand — the hand that wrote a date into Hou Sanliang's counting room in this town.** Not an escape and not a victory: the only one of them that is somebody else's day to keep. She tells nobody, including him. |
| 183 | Run Lists | Days 227–228. The wash house has nothing to copy — the road has been shut a hundred days — and the girl is being paid four hours a week to copy two sheets with a date on them and no road. **She buys an hour of copying that is not on the girl's sheet for four coppers** and finds that the county's notice and the carriers' word do not agree by about a week: seven of forty-one run lists say the pass is not clear after the county says it is open, five say nothing at all. **She does not thank the girl, who arranged that in advance and told three washerwomen about it.** Relief at a free source, immediately followed by the ugliness of buying an hour out of a woman with a bad leg. **New danger.** |
| 184 | One at a Time | Days 229–230. **The back of a dead quarry weight ticket.** She rules a channel to four sentences a day in her own hand, and he corrects five to four in one sentence and never repeats it. The card goes under the oilcloth above the Clay Plate. She tries to keep it in daylight in the yard behind the Hall and cannot: **a woman who has been in the paper of nine counties cannot be seen in a yard holding a book in her own hand.** The only funny thing he says in the batch is not funny. **Decision.** |
| 185 | Say It Slower | Days 231–232. The standing form. He names the price before she asks, **eight days out of about ten**, and gives her about four counts to decide. She says it back slower and does not say the word. **And the sentence she has kept back once a year since the middle of the summer — to say out loud in a room that she has not forgiven him — goes on the price instead**, so that when the year comes round there will be nothing set aside for it. She works out in the dark that she has been keeping a record of an injury in order to say once a year that it had not healed, and that nobody was auditing the record. **Decision.** |
| 186 | Irregular | Days 233–234. **BATCH MIDPOINT.** Nine men read *irregular* on the front of the quarterly for the fourth time in a year. **Gao does the sum out loud a second time and it costs him**, because he tells them the second time is for her and she is standing at the frame. Their hours are on a sheet that is not a claim. She tries to spend what she has — a signature in four county books and eleven minutes with the flat of a second finger — and **both are worth nothing at a frame.** Any man of the crew can sign for a shift; she is not one of the crew. **She is the only person in that hall who cannot pay anybody**, and she writes the Hall's sheet out in eleven minutes in a hall with no roof. **Loss.** |
| 187 | The Price of a Name | Day 235. **BATCH CLIMAX AND ITS IRREVERSIBLE DECISION.** Price named twice, word said once, in the standing form, to nobody, on a mat. It runs less than a day and stops on its own, and he puts it down and does not offer to hold it again. What comes back is **Luo Ping**, thirty-nine, a schedule-man who has kept other people's schedules for sixteen years, with a wife and a boy of eleven who live past this county's boundary. What it did not buy is said in the same breath: not her, not the claim, not the date, not the reason, not the answer on the shelf. Then the flatness after a purchase, and then the dread: **a name is not a ledger entry.** She has bought a person by buying his name, at a price she said out loud first. **Revelation.** |
| 188 | A Man With a Family | Days 236–238, three days on purpose. **The year comes round on a bench in a wash house and there is nothing set aside for it**, because the reserved sentence went on a price. She takes the name to the lane with the wall and stands there four minutes: she could spend everything that is left of him inside the bound and it would not reach a counting room with a list on its door. **She does not go and find him.** She writes him on the back of the card under the column in her own hand and keeps him for the road. What replaces the yearly refusal is not forgiveness — **it is relief**, at not having to prove she is angry to an empty room, immediately followed by the cost, which is that she has just agreed to spend him. **Irreversible decision.** |
| 189 | Member Present | Day 239, the sixteenth, the third hour, the fourth attendance. Four miles down and four miles back in the deep cold, three counts on the stair, eleven minutes at a counter. **Qin is beside her and not eight feet off**, and nobody in the room makes anything of it. Sun He rules the three gaps in the ordinary four words **and does not say the ruling aloud**, because he has said it three times in nineteen months. Bai Zhu tells her, unasked, that the answer is still four words and a blank, and says in the same breath that he is never going to start asking her questions. **She spends a quarter of the day's ration on nine words** — whether he can feel the channel going — and gets a short answer, and has wasted it. **Cost.** |
| 190 | A Bed of Ash | Days 240–243, closing on the thaw being eleven days off. About thirty coppers left, and about six nights of stone in them. **She stops buying, in daylight, at the board, in front of the man who sells it, without bargaining and without saying why.** Da Shan loses four loads a week off a board that has not moved in four years, says the true thing about it, and is not thanked. **Nobody is hurt, nothing breaks, nobody dies.** Qin notices the fire has gone out, fills a bucket himself, and asks nothing — and that is worse than if he had asked. **Six warm nights closed for ten people, to hold thirty coppers for a road.** **Cost.** |
| 191 | What a Question Costs | Days 244–250, seven days. **The ration thins on its own** — four marks a day for a week, then three, then two, then one — because she has been spending him on questions she already knew the answer to. **The Ledger is refused out loud on a mat at the sixth of seven nights, before he can say the price**, and she does not explain why. Then she buys the road out of paper instead: forty-one run lists, and two carriers at a board behind a well, for nothing. What a carrier knows about a road is **loads, days, and a place where a cart turns**, and nothing whatever about anybody. The lists also show that **the carriers do not run past a certain point, because there is nothing on the far side of it for them to run to.** The rule is rewritten on the card: do not buy a thing with him that a carrier will say out loud in a yard. **Emotional consequence.** |
| 192 | One Name and a Column | Days 251–253. She counts everything she is taking, in the yard, in daylight: about thirty coppers under a board she chose not to spend, a licence with nothing above nine on the front of it anywhere in the world, a claim with a date in it, a ruled space still empty, nine men, a girl, a keeper, the plate, and a register still reading *reconciled*. He is counted as **there is not much of him. Enough** — with no figure in it. She works out that she is going east, on purpose, **and not to escape the hunt, which is behind her and out of reach**: she is going because no office inside this county will certify hours worked past its boundary, and the name she bought points that way. She does not pack, does not say goodbye, and does not tell nine men in a speech. **The road opens in the morning. Decision.** |

| 193 | Nine Days Out | Day 254, the morning the season's road reopens. **A carrier came in on the night before with a sheet he wanted copied and the girl of twenty-four copied it, and along the bottom of it, where his own entries had been rubbed out for reuse, somebody had written the course's roll of hands — eleven men, a foreman's name, a day rate, a tender's name, and the working reach: nine days.** That is the only reason a roster has ever reached a Lowwater wash house and it is a job and not a favour; she is not thanked, not paid and not offered anything. She reads the public shelf **once** — the sealed notice giving the eighth of the ninth month, and the district's answer still four words and an empty space the width of a line — and that is the last time in this stretch of days she stands inside reach of a county office. Nine men in a cold room with a bucket somebody has already filled; she says one sentence, makes no speech, and **Gao holds the frame and, instead of saying the sum a third time, tells her the second bunk has not been moved off anything.** Qin walks her to the dressed stone at the county's edge and puts a warm flat of bread into her hand and asks nothing. **She spends one of the day's ration on the grade and it is wasted.** She is out of reach of the square, the shelf, the tower, the wash house and the board behind a well for the rest of the run. **Decision.** |
| 194 | The Fourth Bridge | Days 255–256. A contractor's cart stops under the bridge and she finds out it is for sale. **Twenty-two coppers takes her two days to the turn of the Verge, where the stone turns back; fourteen takes her one day out and no return, and she buys the second one and pays it in coin out loud on the bridge.** The driver tells her a day rate plainly because a man who will not tell you a rate wants something for it: **six coppers a day and a place at the fire.** She does the arithmetic on the tail-board of a moving stone load with a splinter and gets it right twice. **Thirty coppers is five days of the pay at the end of that road, and the cart cost about four, and moved her two days closer — so the passage costs more per day than the work pays.** A carrier knows loads, days, and the four places where a cart turns, and the last turns because there is nothing past it worth an axle; and everything that runs that road is either stone out or paper back, and paper weighs what a leaf weighs. **One ration spent. She gets an answer about a cart and not about a line.** **Cost.** |
| 195 | The Working Reach | Days 257–259, the climb out of the Verge onto the high ground. **Three counts on the flat and four on a grade, and five there and not taken.** It is not stairs: a stair tells the body what is coming and a grade does not. **A body at Condensing nine has a limit and it is not the limit of a person who has done nothing — the fullness does not make the work cheaper, it makes the work heavier, because there is more of her in every thing she lifts**, and that is going to be true of everything she does for the rest of her life. Three mistakes in one day and the correction of each: a boot with stone in it, a hamlet she did not go into for water, and six hours carrying her bag in the arm that was open. **At the worst of the second day, on a long false rise, she wants to know what is past the next rise so badly that her hand is on it — and she does not order it.** One sentence of narration, no dialogue, no price, and nobody told. **The ration goes to nothing for a whole day and comes back the next day at one**, and two whole days at ch 206 have nothing in them at all. She sleeps eight hours a night for the first time since the winter, **and the reason being alone is bearable is that nobody is asking her anything and there is nothing to ration.** **Cost.** |
| 196 | The Field Agent | Days 260–261. The high ground comes down into somebody's country and the change is so gradual she does not find it — **no gate, no post, no clerk, no notice.** Lit windows that are lit because somebody wanted them lit; fields with ditches and gates; a road used rather than maintained, with a nine-inch weed up through the middle of a rut; lime mortar in a ditch because a woman whose name nobody there knows wanted it to hold in a wet year. **In nine days of it, not one person said a word to her**, and she worked out that that was not a courtesy but a mechanism: here there is no clerk, no frame, no trough and no counter, so there is no version of her anywhere. **And announcements do not reach past the bound**, which she thinks about once, in one sentence, and does not turn into a theory. **Bai Zhu is ahead of her on the road**, in a plain coat with a notebook, having walked east ahead of a road he is under no instruction to walk. **He has not asked her a question and says so in the same breath**, as he always has. She spends one ration asking what he is doing here and **it buys the truest thing he has said to her in a volume** — the wages are low, the lodging is worse, nobody will ask her anything, that is not a courtesy, and she will be able to do more there than anywhere, because nobody there wants anything off her that is not her hours. **She does not thank him, because thanking him would make his work into a kindness.** **Revelation.** |
| 197 | The Course | Days 262–263. **Nobody meets her.** The camp goes on around her the way a camp goes on around a cart: fire, kettle, a man mending a boot, eleven men, a foreman of about fifty, a girl of nineteen with her father's hands, and a trestle with a closed book on it. **She stands at the edge with her bag in her hand for the better part of a day and nobody asks her what she wants.** Not a test and not hostility: **a place where people are paid by the day does not care who turns up in the day.** She had a sentence ready for the man with the book and never uses it. She learns one word — **the course** — and does not get inside the wire, because not being on the book is a rule and she intends to respect it until the book has her on it. **Eleven men and the strongest body on the course: she clears a length that the girl of nineteen does in eight minutes and she takes forty, and neither of them says anything.** In the evenings eleven men sit in a ring on the ground and do nothing at all, and about nine hundred words of it go past her every night, and it is the first evening in four years she is not being spoken to and she is so tired of being spoken to that the tiredness comes out of her shoulders. **She waits a whole working morning and does not ask a single person to make her a place.** No ration spent, and she does not say why. **Emotional consequence.** |
| 198 | The Hour It Starts | Day 264, the first working morning. **The foreman puts the book down on the plank and does not open it and asks her for the one thing she has never been able to produce: a name with a county behind it**, because a schedule-man's name is not enough — the book needs a county that can find the hours. He tells her what she is about to buy, in the voice a man reads a line off a board. **She says it in four seconds** and it is the same name she had never been able to put in a place that wanted it, and he has it right the first time. She does the whole arithmetic in about two seconds: before it she was invisible to every office past this boundary; the moment the name is down, her hours go in order with dates into a book that office can read — **which is exactly what a registrar wrote on a bar in the ordinary hand, and exactly what would let a referral be executed.** She says nothing about any of it, says the name first and thinks about it afterwards, **and would do it again in the morning, which is the frightening part and not the finding.** Leg one of three: **a contractor's clearance, an ordinary paper in his own hand with no county stamp and no county number, in a different book from the county one.** Six coppers at the trestle at the end of the day, **and the tin comes out lighter by the evening, because the fire, the board and the bread come out of the same six.** **Irreversible decision.** |
| 199 | The Wage-Book | Day 265, the morning, and the long central chapter. **The foreman puts the book down open in front of her and turns round and walks away, and he is under no obligation to.** Four columns: **date, stretch, hours, price** — and beside every stretch a painted mark, a bar and a cross and a ring, so a mark stands for a place. **The price column is the reason column, and the hours go in it**, and she sees that in four seconds without knowing what it will cost her until the sixteenth. **THE FIRST HONEST DESCRIPTION OF THE LINE IN THIS BOOK**, in a worker's mouth: it is **the line**, it is not stone, it has never been in the same place on both sides of itself, it does not move; **the work is tending and the job is the course**; where the work is done and done well and on time the line holds, where it is not it leaks, and what comes out is **drift**, thicker where the work is worst. The tender adds his own account and says it flatly: what is said inside goes in the ground on the far side and runs along and pools and thickens where the ground is soft — **and that is ninety parts of it, and he has never seen the tenth.** **She is in a room with a man telling her the rules who also has to live inside them, for the first time in four years**, and she can see the proof on sixty yards inside the wire, and she argues with nobody and offers to fix nobody's schedule, and spends nothing of the ration and says why in the chapter. **Respect, and the particular unhappiness of respecting something that is keeping her under.** **Revelation.** |
| 200 | Three Marks On A Sheet | Day 265, the evening, in a room at the west end of the camp. **Lu Kang, Deng Chao and Pei An are out here signing other men's hours, which is the whole of their trade, and there are about a hundred sheets on the table and hers is one of them.** She had walked nine days with a seven-word sentence ready for that room and never uses it. They ask her nothing; nothing is charged; **she says thank you to nobody, and it is not coldness and not pride — a thing done in the ordinary course of a man's trade is not a gift.** One of them tells her unprompted that she has bought a week and not a name, and that the mark is worth what the other ninety-nine are worth. The reason the three of them sign is **not stated, not hinted at and not explained**, and nothing about their second book is lodged or spoken. She writes it on the back of a dead weight ticket in her own hand: **a sheet with three marks on it is worth a week and not a name.** She is disappointed that the room did not cost her something and works out immediately that she had no right to be, because nothing had been offered and she had not asked. **Decision.** |
| 201 | The Office's Hand | Day 266.
