**Volume 5 — Mortal Ash, ch 239–298, outlined in `outline/volume-05.md`. ALL FOUR BATCHES ARE WRITTEN AND REPAIRED, AND THE VOLUME IS CLOSED AND AUDITED — Batch 0001 (ch 239–252), Batch 0002 (ch 253–267), Batch 0003 (ch 268–283) and Batch 0004 (ch 284–298). File set after the volume close and its review repair: 101,266 words of body prose over sixty chapters, three bracketed layer lines, at ch 258, 275 and 292. **Volume 6 is planned and Batch 0001 is carded: `outline/volume-06.md` and `outline/batches/volume-06-batch-0001.md` both exist, and the next phase drafts ch 299–313 against them.**

**Ch 239–252 (Volume 5, batch 0001).** Keeping and being kept. **She works the far reach at five coppers a day and is kept by a wage-book that already holds her.** A stone bank past the post, a ramp, a sledge, eleven men and a man at the ramp called Da, and the far-side wage-book on a plank with **two things in it that matter: a price column that takes the rate said out loud, and a column nearest the near side of the plank that is hers**, in order, with dates, continuous since the fourth of the month before. **She writes the date in herself, about eleven minutes, left hand, flat of a second finger, and the man at the plank holds the page flat with his palm because the plank is not level and does not say her name.** The far-side store is a counter and five coppers and it is **not** the ch 226 store — different building, different country, three-quarters of a mile of the worst ground on nine days between the two, same bread, same price, same hour. **The pay-cycle is established once, out of the book: the seventh, the seventeenth and the twenty-seventh**, ten days to an instalment, three in a month; the pay-days inside this batch are days 480, 490 and 500. **A day not worked is a day not paid, no hours can be moved in either direction by anybody** — she asks the only question she asks anybody in the chapter and is told *there is no such thing* in four words — **and there is nothing to borrow against and nobody to borrow from.** The one total in the batch, stated once and **recognised rather than performed**, because ch 226 already put the same sum at the same price on the page: five in, five out, the number in her hand the same at the start of the day and at the end of it. **The country asks nothing.** Hedged ground, a mill with a wheel that is not turning, a field mowed in a strip a year and left, and **ground past the mill with nothing on it at all, which she has never stood on in her life** — inside a bound every square foot is somebody's. She asks one free question of a man who has no reason to answer it — *who owns the bank* — and gets three sentences about a fact and not about her. **The strongest body within nine days of road is worth five coppers a day and is measured entirely by what she turns over with a bar.** **The physical risk, at the ramp:** she takes the heavy end because nobody is going to give it to her, and on the third day **the cart above the ramp moves while she has the bar under it and nine hundred pounds of wet stone comes down across the hand with four fingers on it.** She gets it clear, nothing breaks, she loses the rest of that day's ramp work, **the day is paid at five coppers like every other day, and there is not one person within nine days of road to tell.** **Wei Ran, twenty, one team, on one card, in the register she is in — a rate and a road and not a favour:** six a load to the mill, eight a day for a man with his own back, four of these loads on a cart, and **ninety coppers for a person and one bag on the road west when it opens — which is shut for the season, named once, by the one person here who would know, with no date from anybody.** She has been quoted it before, at twenty coppers, two months back, and did not take it; she writes the figures on the back of the third dead weight ticket clear of the ration's marks at eleven minutes a line. **The inside-the-bound card, once, at ch 244:** a hauler at the pot, by report and by accident, gives three facts — **a tower with a light in it that goes on at the fourth hour and does not stop; a bundle going down the stair and a clerk's arms going up four flights with it and coming down empty; and the floor is nine men and it is the same nine and they are all still on it.** Nobody at that pot has ever met Feng and does not know his name. **She does not ask a second question, because the only question that would get her more is one whose asking would tell six men that a thing about a tower on the other side of a line is not news to her**, and she writes none of it down. **Midpoint, ch 246:** standing at the plank she works out in four plain sentences that the book on the plank is the only book in the world that holds her as a person with days in it, that the sheet on a spike four hundred yards away is the only other paper within nine days of road with her name on it, that no county has heard of either, **and that a figure is only paid against something and the only hours on this side of the line that can be set against a figure are hers.** Then she walks to the post and back, comes down, and **does not stop working** — the figure was read out loud a month ago in a room with a stove in it whether she was working or not, and the only thing work changes is that it hands the people who can act on it a longer column. **There is no villain to be angry at and she wants one and is not going to get one.** **IRREVERSIBLE, ch 247: she says her name the second time on this ground and is booked to the end of the season** — the second date at the far end of her column in a different hand, the two ends of one line written by two people about forty minutes apart. It costs her the option of leaving on a morning's notice, which was the only option she had that was worth nothing, and **it is a hold she has got and a hook she wanted: she knows the difference and did it anyway, in daylight, nobody persuaded.** **The Hunter, one card, at nine feet, notebook shut against his side:** the standing one-breath form — he has not bought, threatened, touched, entered or asked anything — and then, once, in the ordinary voice, **he does not want the claim answered either**, because an answered claim is an answer somebody above him has to reckon with and he has never believed the reckoning would be the right one, and the same is true of his own order and he has said that before. He is a professional disagreeing with his own office for the second time, and she cannot be angry at him because everything he says is true and he has never once taken anything. **Nine stubs in four marks at ch 249**, laid out on her knee in order: no overlap, no contradiction, no gap. It is the first time she has been able to prove she was somewhere, and the proof is nine strips of cloth written by four men who do not know each other and have never met her. **The cold, at ch 250:** the remedy is free past the post and it is not enough — inside the bound she paid five coppers a day for the right to stand inside eight feet of the same fire — **and the eight feet exist only while eleven men are sitting in them, and at the eighth hour four of them let the fire down without asking anybody.** She wants a pot and a shelf and a fire that is hers, and does not say it to anyone. **CLIMAX, ch 251–252:** she goes into the low room for the first time and asks one ordinary worker-at-a-counter question about whether the work out past the post is written down anywhere else, and the man at the counter — the same room, the same six men, the same stove, the same man who read it out loud a month ago — **reads off a sheet, out loud, at the speed a man reads: the claim is entered in good order, travels with the licence, is not affected by the relicensing, stands, and is actionable on this side, and this is the first office within nine days of road ever in a position to act on it in its whole life.** He does not say her name and does not ask for it. She asks what it would take, and he says it in the ordinary voice: **a claim is a figure, a figure is only paid against something, the only things on this side of the line a claim can be paid against are hours worked past the last working point, and there is one run of those with dates in it on this reach and it is in a book four hundred yards away with a woman's name at the top of it — and that is her column, and nobody in that room is going to take a person's hours off a piece of paper somebody else read out loud in a room, and if she ever says yes they will take them, and until she says it the claim stands and it costs them nothing to stand.** **She says no once, in her own head, in about nine words, with no speech and nobody hearing.** Power: **FOUNDATION 9 for all fourteen chapters, day 471 to day 506. Zero bracketed layer lines in the batch.** Channel: **about six weeks at the open and no figure after it anywhere; six sentences of his across fourteen chapters, at most two in one chapter, all present tense.** Ledger: **not ordered, not held, not priced, not refused — hers to order and she does not order it.** Merit: **zero; nothing killed and nothing cleared.** Claim: **standing, eight hundred jin, actionable on the far side, not answered, not paid, not assessed; Hou Sanliang unpaid, patient, never met; the date in Lowwater untouched; no draft written and none sent.**

**Ch 253–267 (Volume 5, batch 0002).** Debt coming due. **The road west reopens on day 507 and a draft goes west on a carrier's round with no date on it.** Wei Ran gives the road once, in her own register: ninety coppers for a person and one bag, nine days hard going, the coming a different number nobody quotes. **The claim's second movement begins without being answered:** the office sends a young clerk to the trestle and then a mild man to the store, and the price is said three times in three rooms with three voices — her column, and nobody takes it until she says it — and **she refuses three times, once in her head and twice out loud, and Hou stays unpaid and unmet and the date in Lowwater untouched.** **IRREVERSIBLE, ch 255: she takes furnace brick, wire, a crucible and clay at twenty-nine coppers on promise against her booked pays, money she has not got, closing the road west behind her for the season.** A listed furnace-tending job cleared by hand in a flooded shed settles merit once, told before spent, yes said, intercepted out of her foundation, two holds gone and not coming back; Ledger not ordered and not touched. **Golden Core 1 at ch 258, day 512, at her own order, in the open, where nothing is announced** — fifty days after the ninth, forty-one after the volume's first day — with the volume's first bracketed layer line since ch 237, and the cost is the turn at the end of a bar stroke, gone permanently, one-handed bar work done. **Feng's fair Foundation copy past the line at ch 262, Feng never on the page:** his hand, one through nine with one hole, up a stair that belongs to a district clerk and into a far-side book, taken by the keeper's paste without knowing what it is. She leaves the reach on the reopened road for a day and comes back inside the yard fence, costing the day's pay. Inside the bound once, by report only: a tower lit, a bundle on a stair, nine men still on the floor. **The surface answer paid once, at ch 267, in a worker's mouth, in short sentences: being told and being charged were never the same thing** — a stage told nine days west rides the drift and goes on the board as a reading, and a stage taken past the line goes in the ground and reaches no county book. The tenth part not given; the crews not proved wrong. Power: **Golden Core 1 (ch 258). One bracketed layer line in fifteen chapters, at ch 258, and none elsewhere.** Eleven stubs in four marks; nine holds; four fingers; a furnace of her own; booked to the end of the season. **REVIEWED AND REPAIRED:** **25,441 words of body prose over fifteen chapters, average ~1,696**, day 507 to day 521, against 21,450 and ~1,430 before repair, when the batch fell monotonically from 1,908 to 1,349. Two duplicated paragraphs removed (ch 264, ch 267); the *three months back* interval corrected to counted days in three chapters and four more (**fifty days** at ch 258, **fifty-two** at ch 260, **fifty-nine** at ch 267, all measured from ch 237's day 462**); one local repetition cut; **ch 262's section order repaired and its doubled plank entry removed**; **ch 257's shed transaction and ch 266's season count made to agree with every other chapter**; 3,932 words added inside existing beats to close the decline. **No plot moved: the fifteen titles, the chapter order, the day table, every pinned beat, the single bracketed layer line and the surface answer at ch 267 all stand.** Full reasoning in `state/continuity.md` under *Volume 5 Batch 0002 — REVIEW REPAIR*.

**Ch 268–283 (Volume 5, batch 0003).** Family paper. **Day 522 to day 580, sixteen chapters, ONE bracketed layer line, at ch 275.** **IRREVERSIBLE, ch 268, cold and unplanted: a boy from the mill end says six words on a bank, and in a low building four hundred yards past the post a room offers to take the eight hundred jin onto its own book against hours worked past the last working point. It costs her nothing until it is answered, and it is hers to close. She says yes in daylight, and the pen moves in the ordinary way, and the bound's bill closes by opening one the bound cannot read. From that hour leaving costs the far side instead of the bound, and no instrument she can think of can undo it.** She walks nine days west on the thirtieth with ninety coppers and a bag, misses the seventeenth and the twenty-seventh standing as gaps in a column four hundred yards behind her, and crosses a plank bridge gone soft at the fixings with the paper high on her back and nobody within a day's walk in either direction. **The return inside happens once, at ch 271 on the ninth: a floor, nine men and the same nine, a wash house with a girl of twenty-four whose leg holds her weight now, a tower with a light in it that burns in daylight. She stands one attendance she has already lost, in person, in her own hand, and puts a paper for her brother's name on the board under the date, and asks for one hour and gets it. Then Lowwater, where she stands in the lee of a stranger's doorway outside Hou Sanliang's counting room and does not knock, and works out that the hand which put the date in is the hand that will take it out, and that it is not hers and will not be done in a season she can walk to.** **CH 272, *The Man the Board Keeps*, close third on Yuan, the batch's flagged exception: a keeper of about fifty-five puts up a strip wet with paste nine or ten days whether or not there is anything in it, and offers to enter him as a readable line so her hours hold past the line without her. The price is on the table first: his unentered state and the date of the morning he died. He keeps the apology and loses the day. He wants her hours, specifically, because that run is the only thing standing between eight hundred jin and a man she left standing in a county that could not pay it — and being read means being findable, and he has never been anywhere without her, and he does the arithmetic anyway and steps into the line.** The Ledger stops holding him as a standing in a book. What holds him now is a strip of cloth under a lump of paste, and it does not need her to look at it. **Then Golden Core 2, ch 275, on the third of the month, day 556, forty-four days after the first stage, at her own order, in the open, where nothing is announced.** The second layer line of the volume. **The cost: the flat of the second finger goes from eleven minutes a line to longer, permanently, and every hour she writes after it costs her the hour it is written in.** She writes slower for the rest of her life. **Ch 276–283 carry it.** The furnace held banked twenty-one days and she tests it with the back of her wrist twice. Ning Bo's account is **worked and not settled** — a hauler says once on the yard that the paper on the board has not been taken down, and an old mill worker of sixty says once on a bank that old accounts close worked, and she understands on the sixth that she has done the part she can do and the rest is his doing. **Ning Wu's decade is reachable and not met** — the woman with the scarred forearm says once that a carrier's list west carries ten years of a name that is not crew and not floor. The woman with the scarred forearm moves one place along the stones at the fire, once or twice a chapter, without asking, and never says the second thing. **A slab comes off the wet bank toward her foot and goes past her boot: eleven slabs in two months and one missed by the width of a boot.** **A pay clerk runs four columns and puts a thumb down the side of hers and says nothing about the ten gaps in it.** The register **still reads *reconciled* and is not corrected** — a lie with a signature under it, in a book that has never held her brother, and a page closed with a signature under the closing cannot be opened by any clerk with a dry hand. **At ch 281 the keeper puts the strip up and she watches it from forty yards and does not go up, because going up would put her next to a man who has just been made findable, and there would be nothing afterwards that could put it back.** **Fifteen stubs in four marks, none checkable within nine days of road, and a far-side book holding an eight hundred jin figure against hours she works past the line.** Power: **Golden Core 2.** One bracket in sixteen chapters, at ch 275, matching the pinned text character for character; nothing above the Standing Office of the Second Account named; no new villain; no month or weekday named. **REVIEWED AND REPAIRED: 22,771 words of body prose, average ~1,423, shortest ch 277 at 1,341, longest ch 272 at 1,649, none under the 1,300 floor**, against 736–1,416 and ~1,076 before repair. Authorial scaffolding, an ending preview and three forbidden tier names cut from ch 272; the day table corrected so **Golden Core 2 lands on the pinned day 556** and ch 292's Golden Core 3 is correctly forty-four days after it; the nine-day walk reconciled at both ends; the twenty-chapter identical Yuan tag broken across sixteen chapters; two reversed day-orders and one duplicated sentence fixed. **No beat, date, stage, title, bracket or planned event moved.** Full reasoning in `state/continuity.md` under *Volume 5 Batch 0003 — REVIEW REPAIR*.

**Ch 284–298 (Volume 5, batch 0004).** Ash and leaving. **Day 581 to day 620, fifteen chapters, ONE bracketed layer line, at ch 292 — the third and last of the volume.** **The furnace goes out in the night on the twenty-eighth, for the first time in twenty-five days, and the ground under it gives nothing back: sixty-nine days of heat gone into a hole in a field four hundred yards east of a post, and she has never once said that out loud and never once will.** She relights it at the third hour and loses the morning, because a bank waits for nobody. **The course she cut: she will not buy on a promise a second time, so she takes two days out of the column and lays the seam herself — sixty-one bricks on edge instead of flat, twice the width, out of clay dug by hand out of the yard's own low bank, and the two days go into the column in plain ink in front of the man at the plank, which is the only currency she has, because she has not been hungry since the first Foundation and every copper she earns has exactly one use.** **The far date, ch 286: she goes and reads the date at the end of her column for the first time in a hundred and sixteen days of going round it four inches short, and it is the thirtieth. Twenty-six days. And the three days before it are working days and the next pay-day is the seventh of the next month, so the twenty-eighth, the twenty-ninth and the thirtieth are fifteen coppers she will work and never be paid for.** The office tells her what it needs from her in the plainest sentence of the batch: *a figure is a figure and it is only paid against something; if the something has a start and an end and is true all the way through, I can write it out and send it.* **Pay-day the seventh, the sixteenth stub.** Da puts out the reach's last run — a low bank at the end of the reach, nobody hired on it, four days for a crew, off the ground before the last cart or the season's last run does not count — and gives her the true reason in the ordinary voice: a crew would want more than five coppers for undercut ground at the end of a season and he cannot pay more and will not sign a man to it. She takes it off **and it is not in his book and she is not paid for it**, and she promises him one thing and keeps it. **The face lets go, ch 288: the whole line of it, at once, at the fifth hour, with her in the cut to the thigh and the bar under the falling lip.** Eleven counts. She gets out with the bar and four fingers and nothing else, and nobody within a day's walk comes, because nobody is within a day's walk. The right arm goes from open a third of the time to open half, and does not go back. The ramp is closed and the heavy end is gone for good at that yard.** Da puts two men on the clearing out of the season and says why, and it is not kindness: a face that has let go once will let go again and he is not having a man under it at the end of a season for a bank nobody hired on. **The yard rearranges itself around a woman who cannot lift without a word being said about it, and the man from the mill end works a row out to the bar at her pace and then says: *so it is worth doing now*, and goes to his end. That is the whole account eleven men take of the fact that she is getting weaker at the only thing she could do.** **The season's last stone goes on a cart, the listed run closes, and merit settles a second time on a far-side wage-work and not on a death.** Wei Ran, one card, in her own register: eight a load past the cart turn where carts are short, take it below eight and you are being paid what a two-handed woman is paid for a thing a two-handed woman does — and she does not take it, because a column that restarts mid-season is a column with a hole in it in front of everybody, and she put two holes in that column herself in plain ink. **Told before spent, ch 290:** he takes a share, holds it, and names the price before he moves — one line in the record the offices read, saying the balance carried forward against her is discharged and nothing is carried against her name — and the price is her foundation and **not one person alive has ever been able to tell her how much, including him.** She does not say no. That is all it is: she does not say no, and she does not say yes, and she does not thank him. **Golden Core 3, ch 292, on the seventeenth, day 600 — a pay-day, and forty-four days after the second stage — taken at her own order in daylight in the middle of a reach where eleven men are two hundred yards behind her.** The third and last bracket of the volume. **The cost is the first one that is not on her body at all: nothing in the breath, the arm, the finger, the holds, the aperture, the crack, the writing hour. She goes through the whole list at the second hour and finds it true.** From that hour she is a person an office will vouch for, entered as good in a book the bound cannot read, **and she cannot be un-entered** — and she asked, and the man behind the counter told her a book does not give a thing back and he would be telling her the same thing in a year. **The entry is not read out: an entry that answers nothing gets written down, and the difference between that and the seventh being read aloud in this room once is the whole distance between an office that can act and an office that can only keep.** **She is glad of it, and the gladness is the frightening part: she has spent five months being illegible and a word about her in ink is water in a dry place, and she has heard the word good in four rooms and it has cost a person something every time.** The Hunter's notebook is on the low table in that room and has never once had her name in it, and that is the only protection she has against every office in nine days of road and it belongs to a man she has never thanked. **Ch 294, the answer:** she goes up at the second hour on the twenty-second because there are eight days left and an office told her plainly what it needed, and the man behind the counter **reads the answer out once, in a room with nine men in it, and it is the seventh condition discharged and the seven conditions are never re-listed** — the figure, the run from the second of a month four months back to the thirtieth, twelve gaps entered by the wage-holder and true, the wage-holder entered as good, eight hundred jin payable in Lowwater at the counting room of Hou Sanliang and nowhere else. **The draft goes west the same day, on the round, with no date on it, because a dated draft is held for a date.** Irreversible and un-un-answerable. **And nobody inside nine days of road will ever hear that it closed**: nine counties heard nothing, four counties wrote nothing, and a county book has no column for a thing done past its own boundary. **Ch 295, nine days of waiting and the word.** The pay-day of the twenty-seventh is the last one — the eighteenth stub. The three unpaid days. The column closed at the thirtieth and the book going into the box at the mill. **Then the hauler, once, without a second question: the draft came into Lowwater, took it, paid the figure — eight hundred — and the date came off the page. Not struck out. Off.** Not a clerk's hand: a clerk strikes out and writes beside it why, and a schedule-man does not sit in a room with his own hand on a lie in a book. **Hou Sanliang is paid and never met and never going to be met. The hand that took the date off is the hand that wrote it, and she bought that name once for eight days of a man's life and has never spoken to him.** She wanted the eighth question and did not ask it. **Ch 296, the family account, by his doing:** he spends it on the second and she feels it go and then spends two days looking for it and finds nothing — breath, arm, finger, holds, aperture, writing hour, all true, none of it different — **because the Foundation tax is the one cost in five volumes that leaves nothing to point at, and she is going to carry it for the rest of her life with no instrument that will ever tell her whether it was large.** On the fourth she goes to the counter and he turns the book round: *Balance carried forward, Ning Yan, of Lowwater, against the furnace account of Ning Bo, discharged. Six years carried. No balance standing. Nothing carried against the name.* The office entered it because it answers something on its own page — *an office enters a thing that makes a thing on its own page true. That is not discretion. That is the whole of the job* — and what it does in Lowwater is **nothing**, because no instrument in nine days of road reaches a county. **The account is closed where it can be closed.** And the cost she cannot name is not the price: it is that the account had been there since she was eleven, and every single thing that had ever been taken off her had been taken against it, and the taking had been a payment and not a robbery, **and there is now nothing left to pay it on, and the last page in the world with her father's name on it is four hundred yards behind her in a country the name never travelled to.** **Ch 297:** she goes up and asks for a line. A name, a county, ten years — assessed at the Broken Kiln, reassigned above furnace authority, ten years on a carriers' list that is not crew and not floor, kept by somebody whose name the woman who told her did not know, ten years of his own. The clerk says that is not how a line goes in this book and she says yours have dates at the left and hours against them and yours need this more than they need the ninth run, and he writes it in the ordinary hand with the date in the margin and **no mark on it, because a mark on it would make it an entry about him**, and he does not read it out. **The list is nine days of road away, or further, in hands whose keeper she cannot name; the line is four hundred yards behind her. It is not enough, she knows that before she asks for it, and she asked for it anyway.** Bai Zhu at nine feet off the track, one true sentence, no question, no name on anything he has written, and she does not thank him and does not tell him to stop and the year carries. **Ch 298, Mortal Ash:** the seventh is a pay-day and there is no queue and no strip and the book shuts at the dark and goes in the box at the mill. **The first thing she has to do in a season of her own with nothing in it is nothing, and she does not know how to do nothing.** She counts what is true. The register reads *reconciled* and she thinks about it once in the sun and lets it go. **She opens the ring for the last time and spreads the dead ash on the flat stone and looks at it — everything she ever paid went into a hole in a field and the field kept none of it — and she shakes it off her coat and it falls the way ash falls and does not settle and she leaves it where it falls.** The ash of a bound that burned its books to keep a ceiling at nine, and the ash of a ring of brick and clay and wire four hundred yards behind her, are the same trade and she has been inside both. **Nine days of road behind her, walked twice, never again. Nothing inside nine days of road has ever known she existed and nothing on that reach can ask her a question, and both of those were worth a great deal and she cannot say what they cost. She is standing in a place where nothing is said when it happens, with a furnace of her own, and it is the first silence she has ever stood in that is hers.** She walks out past the cart turn toward a mill whose wheel has not turned since before she came, **stops half a mile short of it, finds she is not afraid of it, and is frightened by that.** Power: **Golden Core 3.** One bracket in fifteen chapters, at ch 292, matching the pinned text character for character. Merit settles twice in the volume and neither time on a death; nothing kills anybody. Eighteen counterfoils in four marks at the last page and no nineteenth. The register still reads *reconciled* and was read once and never corrected. The Clay Plate worn and never described. MEMBER ABSENT three times and unraised. The year of non-forgiveness carrying and untouched. Feng never on the page; no second Lung, no second appointment; nothing above the Standing Office of the Second Account named; no new enemy.

**Volume 4 — The Wall, ch 179–238, outlined in `outline/volume-04.md`; all four batches WRITTEN AND THE VOLUME CLOSED AND AUDITED — Batch 0001 (ch 179–192), Batch 0002 (ch 193–208), Batch 0003 (ch 209–222), Batch 0004 (ch 223–238). Four faults repaired at the close and five findings recorded with a reason; the fifteen required continuity facts are confirmed. The next phase is the Volume 5 planning phase.** Where it opened, on the page at ch 179:** day 220, the first week of the deep cold, the crew quarters, ten sleepers in twelve bunks, the second licence nailed straight above the second bunk with seven conditions on the back and nothing above Condensing nine on the front of it anywhere in the world. **A district's slip is pinned to the public shelf by the door and it ends in an empty space the width of a line where an answer was meant to go.** A referral has gone up the stair past every office she has ever stood in front of and the dismantling it orders **can only be executed past a boundary no county paper crosses.** A petition for the relicensing that would carry her to Foundation the first stage is **entered, numbered, barred and not withdrawable**, on the ground that this county keeps no schedule of work done past its boundary. And a man who has never lied to her says there is **about ten days** of him left, against about six weeks of winter still to come in it. **She has the Ledger to order and she does not order it. She does not forgive him and she does not tell him to stop.** The season's road has been shut since day 117 and opens at about day 254. **That is where ch 179 finds it. Batch 0002 opened on the road opening, at day 254, and ends at day 280 with her relicensed above Condensing nine at Foundation the first stage — announced at her own order and heard by nobody at all, because she is nine days outside the bound — in debt of a monthly attendance she missed on purpose and entered against her permanently, working on a line of work kept by hand by people who are right, and holding four stubs of paper off a contractor's book, which is the only object in the world that can say she worked there.**

**Ch 193–208 (Volume 4, batch 0002).** Hiring, the road, and whose book an hour goes into. **The road opens on the morning of day 254 and she is on it before the light is off the hill, on purpose, with about thirty coppers and no speech made to nine men** — and the only reason she knows anything at all about the end of that road is that a girl of twenty-four is paid four coppers a week to copy carriers' lists nobody wants, and a carrier's sheet gets reused. **Nine days out, one way. A contractor's cart at the fourth bridge costs twenty-two coppers for two days; the day rate at the end of it is six and the passage costs more per day than the wage pays.** Nine days of hard road on a body at Condensing nine that is not the limit of a person who has done nothing, and **at the worst of the second day her hand is on the Ledger and she does not order it.** Past the boundary is somebody's ordinary country — lit windows that are lit because somebody wanted them lit, ditches with lime mortar in them, a road used and not maintained — **and nothing is announced out here because nothing is announced out here**, and a field agent who walked east ahead of her buys nothing, asks nothing and says the truest thing anybody has said to her in a year, and is not thanked for it. **She arrives at a camp where nobody meets her and nobody asks her what she wants, because a place where people are paid by the day does not care who turns up in the day**, and stands at the edge of it for a day and a half being the strongest body on the course and the least useful person on it. Then a foreman of about fifty asks for **a name with a county behind it**, and she says it in four seconds, and understands in about two seconds that she has just made herself findable by the one office that can act on an eight hundred jin claim against her — **and would do it again in the morning, which is the frightening part and not the finding.** **The wage-book is held open in front of her by a man under no obligation to, and the first honest description of the line in this book happens there, in a worker's mouth**: it is the line, the work is tending, the job is the course, it holds where it is kept and leaks where it is not, and what comes out is drift, thicker where the work is worst — **ninety parts true, given by people who are right, and not answered further.** **The price column is the reason column and the hours go in it**, and she sees that in four seconds and does not know what it will cost her. Three sponsors sign her sheet in a room with a hundred sheets on the table and charge nothing and ask nothing and are not thanked. **The third leg is countersigned in an office's hand and not a person's, on a schedule read aloud out of a contractor's book by a clerk who is not obliged to show it to her and does not**, and she stands in the room and is not in the middle of it and is nine seconds from reading the number and does not, because a relicensing needs a schedule and not a figure a woman saw once. **The bar a registrar wrote in the ordinary hand was not an obstacle; it was a description of where she was standing.** The drift is thickest where the work is worst and she is the strongest body on that course and has nothing to offer, and a man who keeps other people's schedules is found at work and answers two true questions and is a trade and not a person, and finding him answers nothing. **The tin is gone by the evening of day 268 and the last west-going carrier went eleven days ago and the next one is not this week.** **And on the sixteenth the county's monthly member sheet comes round with the carriers, because a member sheet travels with the work, and it has her name on it and MEMBER ABSENT in the attendance column in the county's own two words — beside her hours, in a column that is not joined to the first, in order, with dates, every one of them good. She is offered the one word that could go in the reason column and does not write it, because the reason column is the column her hours go in.** Six days of a body going cold in a place it has never been cold in, never hungry, never going to be full and warm at the same time again. **And on day 276 Foundation 1 is relicensed and announced at her own order, in the ordinary way, and goes about two hundred yards into a field: nine counties is what is heard and nine counties have heard nothing this year, four is what is written and the four books that would carry it are nine days west and have not been told. It is not a failure. There is a room and she is not in it.** Power: **Condensing 9 for fifteen chapters and Foundation 1 at ch 207, announced and heard by nobody.** Ledger not ordered and no price named; Merit none; he does not grow back and is quieter at the end than at the start. **She ends it relicensed, in debt, working, holding four counterfoils — the only object in the world that can say she was there — having seen three hundred yards of a nine-day line.**

**Ch 209–222 (Volume 4, batch 0003).** The line walked, and the people who live on it. **A course of nine men and a girl, a foreman of about fifty with a daughter on the line, and a tender of about thirty with nine years of a board — and the first honest description of what the thing they maintain actually is, said in a worker's mouth over a pay-book, and it is ninety parts true and this volume does not give the other ten.** **She works the line.** The daily business of it: the marks, the ring, the drift, the eleventh-hour hour, the low side that goes over a boot in four places, the readings a tender copies and a woman copies with the flat of a second finger at eleven minutes a line where he takes four. **The noise and the quiet.** Thick drift under a bar rings and keeps ringing for about as long as it takes to count eleven, and eleven men and a girl have an ordinary working word for it — *a stretch that has got loud* — and they say it as a fact about ground. She keeps sixteen days of the board against what nine counties have heard in order over four years: **the loud stretches are nearest the inside end, the worst is the worst-worked, and the quietest ground on nine days is at the far end, which is where there is nothing to announce because there is nobody there.** She says it to herself once and builds no theory on it and **tells nobody, including him**, because the only control in the world is herself. **THE LAST COLUMN, at ch 216: a narrower column at the far end of the far plank with no heading she can read and nothing on that course that fills it, filled by a strip off a cart from the far end of the working reach, nine years, a hand from past the last stake, a cart not identified on that board.** She can read the frame and not one word inside it. **One line in it is dated the day she took the second Foundation and is in a form none of the nine years around it is in**, and she is the only person within nine days of road who can tell that line from a reading, and she has told nobody she told the difference and has not said out loud what the line is. **Bai Zhu's win, and he does not want it:** he walked nine days east because his order is executable here and nowhere else on earth, and on the eleventh of a month he said in front of her that **he does not want it executed and wants the answer pending**, because a pending answer is a question somebody above him has to answer and he has never once believed the answer would be the right one. Then the line's own people closed their course against her anyway, lawfully, correctly, using his referral, **in a room she was not in.** At the second hour of the seventeenth of the month a foreman came out with the book and **drew one line through her name in the open, in daylight, with six men within thirty feet.** **The hours stand. The rate stops. The book does not close.** **And what it costs them, in arithmetic and not in a speech:** the stretch she kept went back in three days, the run is shorter, the contract is smaller, the board has a hole in it, nine men are idle and the girl of nineteen is idle and she is somebody's daughter. **They are right and nothing in this volume proves them wrong.** Then the batch's irreversible decision: past the last stake there is a **sub-contractor** — not answerable to the foreman, a book of his own, four columns, a painted mark, **five coppers against six and the difference is never explained** — and **she hires on in daylight at his trestle with her name said out loud for the second time on that ground and nobody persuaded. From that hour two books in two hands hold her name, neither is answerable to the other, and the hours split so that neither carries a continuous run — the exact instrument the whole relicensing was built out of, which she took apart with her own mouth eleven days after paying for it.** She does the arithmetic and **does not do it out loud.** And a woman with one team, **Wei Ran, twenty, mortal-born, the first person Ning Yan has met who went through without permission**, says one true thing about the line as a fact and not as comfort and goes off page. Power: **Foundation 2–5, four stages, four brackets, four costs, and not one of them the same thing.** MEMBER ABSENT entered a second time, permanent, beside her hours, in a column not joined to them. The road west closed for the season: **after the carrier who came back on the third, no date west on any board for the rest of the season.** The district's answer space took a date and one office word: **Held.** Nothing was answered.

**Ch 223–238 (Volume 4, batch 0004). Crossing and what it costs. — VOLUME 4 CLOSED.** Nine mornings into a sub-contractor's book at five coppers with bread bought at a store and no share in the fire, **and she is the strongest body on nine days and the slowest hand on a stretch that carries somebody else's mark.** **Foundation 6, and the cost is that the sound in their ears becomes a line on a board and the people on the line can tell a stage from a bill** — nine men look up, the girl of nineteen is the only one who does not, and a tender puts his hand flat on a board and takes it off again. **MEMBER ABSENT a third time, permanent, a reason column a foot and a half wide going west with the last strip of paper that will leave that reach this season, and the word offered twice by two decent men and never a third time.** The bad flat described in full: **no mark, no stake, no board, no ring, a foot and a bit on the low side, two men in it four inches deep with a bar laid across them, two men and a day and a half to walk it, and the shortage was never strength — it is how long a person can be wrong in it and not go in.** **Foundation 7, taken on the first of three days that no book can find, and eleven men stand a stretch for nothing and tell her nothing.** Three empty dates go into the sub-contractor's book and stay empty, because **a hole you cannot see is worse than one you can**, and he squares the book over the top of them. **The price is stated before the word — one day out of three — and then she says it.** **She hires onto the stretch nobody is hired on, at a trestle, in daylight, with her name said out loud for the third time on that ground and nobody persuaded**, and a man tells her three true things about it, including **that if she goes into that low side and it does not come up he will not be the man who stands a stretch on it.** **Foundation 8 is suppressed at her own order and there is no bracketed line in that chapter at all — and for the first time the absence is on a board the far side keeps, and she has put a hole on a board she cannot read.** **She works fourteen days on the blind run by hand, alone, with a bar, and on the fifteenth she goes in to the hip and gets up on her hands**, and on the twenty-first she lays the last mark in lime. **Merit settles on the finished job and not on a death; he takes a share and spends it out of a foundation one stage short of finished and it does not come back — and the right index finger goes, permanently, and she tells the crew out loud before they can work out whether she is going to lose the other one.** **The honest partial answer is said once, against a board, in a worker's mouth, and it is ninety parts true**, and **he reads the office name above this county out loud in one breath — the Standing Office of the Second Account, a middle office with others above it — and stops.** **Foundation 9 is announced at her own order in a place where nothing is announced, and the drift carries it a mile past the last working point, and a keeper of about fifty-five writes it down on his board and does not ask her what the sound was.** **And a claim of eight hundred jin is read out loud in a room on the far side that can act on it, and nobody in that room knows she is standing outside the door.** Then the break, in this order: **the layer logs a dated crossing into a board that was already on the far side, in a column that is hers, and he cannot see her go; and then her body, three-quarters of a mile of quiet ground and a day and a half.** **Bai Zhu certifies it in a Warden's hand, signed by the office and not by a person.** **She writes her name in a book on the far side and says the price out loud in the ordinary voice because that is what is said on a line, and the man writes it in the price column and does not look up, and behind her a board on a post has nine lines in it, eight written and one with nothing on it at all.** Nine days of road behind her, a floor, nine men, and a tower with a light in it. **Nothing follows it.** Power: **Foundation 9, nothing above nine anywhere in the world.** Channel: **about six weeks.** Ledger: **taken twice in Volume 4 and refused once; hers to order at the last page.** **She does not forgive him and she does not tell him to stop.**

## Volume 5 closed — audited

**`outline/volume-05.md` walked line by line against ch 239–298. Ten repairs repaired in the chapters — ten
categories, twenty individual faults, across nineteen reopened chapters and thirty-one chapter files — eight
findings recorded and not repaired, five state-file claims corrected. File set after repair: 101,266 words of
body prose over sixty chapters (101,204 after the close, 101,160 before). **Per batch: 25,897 · 25,441 ·
22,771 · 27,157 — the close refreshed the volume total but left the four batch totals at their pre-close values,
which the close review corrected.** Full table, the count derivation and evidence: `state/current.md`, *Volume 5
close audit — CLOSED*. Reasoning: `state/continuity.md`, *Volume 5 close — repairs* and *Volume 5 close — REVIEW
REPAIR*.

**The ten repairs.** ch 248 — a duplicated paragraph with ch 242 · ch 275 — two duplicated paragraphs with
ch 258 seventeen chapters apart, with **the order's own words kept identical in both chapters**, because the
repetition of them is the point · ch 295 — a duplicated pay-day paragraph with ch 287 · **ch 278 — a named
calendar month (*in April*) → *in the middle of the season*** · **ch 251 — a wrong interval carried out of
Batch 0002 unapplied (*three months ago*) → *a month and a half back*, against ch 236 at day 459 and ch 251 at
days 503–504** · **seven instances of authorial scaffolding at ch 256, 271, 283, 290, 292, 295 and 297**
(*for the rest of the book*, *in this book*, *five volumes*, *four volumes*), against an explicit prompt rule
and a construction budget that recorded the count as zero · **ch 298 — the surface answer re-voiced in the
final chapter, in two sentences, against the pin that it is paid once at ch 267 and never again; both cut and
the section now carries the private knowing without the answer** · **ch 286 and ch 291 — *about four seconds*,
a construction Volume 4 retired, four times in Batch 0004 against a recorded zero; recast at two, kept at 290
and 293 deliberately** · **ch 239–252 — forty-one straight ASCII apostrophes in all fourteen files of Batch
0001, converted; Volume 5 is now typographically uniform** · **ch 261, 262, 264, 265 and 266 — the Yuan tag,
five times byte-identical; five recast at the close review so that no string in the run stands more than twice,
with the device kept and no line of his dialogue touched.**

**The ruling behind the last of them.** The close had cut seven scaffolding instances for being talk about the
book's own construction while recording the tag as an authorised motif, and the two positions could not both
stand. **The tag is not scaffolding** — it names a diegetic register rule (his speech is in the present tense)
and a dramatic fact about the scene (the line bought nothing), never the novel's length, its volumes or its
reader — so **the device was kept and the run was repaired instead.** *(The close's finding counted the verbatim
string at four and named ch 253 by mistake; it is five, at ch 253, 261, 262, 264 and 267.)*

**Confirmed still true after repair.** Three bracketed layer lines in sixty, at 258 / 275 / 292, each matching
the pinned text character for character and no other square-bracketed line in the volume. Zero named weekdays,
zero named calendar months, zero tribulations, zero *kiln-born*, zero MEMBER ABSENT, zero Anchor, zero
Sisterhood, zero Scribe. No authorial scaffolding. No straight apostrophe and no straight double quote anywhere
in ch 239–298. No bold in the volume; twelve italic spans, all documentary. Every file closes every speech. No
paragraph over 99 words. No chapter under the 1,300 floor or above the 2,700 guide.

**Recorded and not repaired.** The Yuan tag (*He said one thing that night and it was present tense and it
bought nothing*) — four verbatim at 261, 262, 264, 267 and three *evening* variants at 263, 265, 266, the
whole of Batch 0002, zero in 268–298; a ledger line, never a chapter's closing line, each utterance different ·
the present-tense documentary block at ch 297 · Wei Ran on four cards where the outline names two batches ·
Bai Zhu named aloud once · *reconciled* read seven times and corrected never · the four weeks every foundation
costs her never stated, because stating it names what `outline/ending.md` §8 reserves for ch 1000.

**Carried forward as proposed repairs for a phase that may reopen closed chapters:** the straight-apostrophe
sweep across Volumes 1–4 (**1,588 instances**) and the named calendar months still standing in closed Volumes
2 and 3 (**fifteen**). Neither is to be done as a side effect of drafting.

**The protagonist, answered.** Yuan is the book's protagonist and Volume 5 put him on the page in his own right
at **ch 272** — he let a keeper's hand enter him as a readable line so her hours would hold past the line
without her, and paid for it with **his unentered state and the date of the morning he died**, both named on
the page before the line went in. **He is now readable from outside the bound without her and therefore
findable, and the chapter said so in his own frame: *being read means being findable… an office that looks at
a line sends for the line.*** `outline/ending.md` §1 needs exactly this before the final volume opens. **He is
not stronger, not restored, not located, not protected and not forgiven. The Ledger is no longer hers to order
and there is nothing she can order instead.**


## Rolling volume summary

**Ch 1–58 (Volume 1, closed).** A girl who was a number in a column gets her name into a hall on purpose, and pays for it in eight more years billed to her dead father, a lost arm, a bought lie, and a bounty on the only person inside the bound who can hear the voice. Yuan spent four seconds to make her too public to be taken quietly and did not ask; she worked it out from the rule alone, did not forgive him, and stayed. A man of sixty-one lost forty-one years for eleven minutes on a technicality so her rank would survive, and walked out without a word. The Hall has no roof and never gets one. Power: Body-Forging 3 → Condensing 1, right arm lost, aperture permanently cracked and stabilised as a mouth.

**Ch 59–72 (Volume 2, batch 0006).** The pass ices and she winters on the mountain with a price on her head and a captain's unasked line in his own book. She learns the name of the only paper that will let her practise — the Licence to Practise Past Registration, Clause Four, three legs, issued at Lowwater — and gets one of the three, and pays the plate clause for it. She works a one-handed tithe in an enemy's ledger at a floor four feet from the fire. Two friends sign her shift in front of eleven witnesses; the sponsors' inspectors come for her bunk; she refuses on a consent regulation that is real, narrow, and does not mention whose fault the roster becomes — and eleven seconds later a foreman puts two names on the settling floor, and a beam she saw the hairline in comes down on them. She names both causes out loud where nobody can soften them, writes them into a register in her own hand, and goes back to the channel. Power: **still Condensing 1.** Two crew dead, ten sleepers in a twelve-bunk room, an application refused once and never again, and a man in a good coat buying proof by name in a market eleven days down the road.

**Ch 73–88 (Volume 2, batch 0007).** Four months of tithe counted slowly in an enemy's ledger, 211 hours to 940, her own signed line continuous and complete. She arranges a lawful descent and then does not use it for four weeks, because a stage is coming and a form is not ready and a body on a square buys nothing. Condensing 2 arrives on the settling floor and is announced without her order; fever and short breath are the price. She orders Yuan to read the buyer's man through her eyes and pays for it. A carrier's letter tells her the county wants a name, and the man in the good coat tells her, by way of a clerk's brush, that he is buying the **absence** of a name: a page in a county file saying no household in this town has her. The county is the only room that can answer it. She rides down on day 58, works carrying loads past three desks and one counting lane, learns that the tower cannot write her down without handing her upward, and goes back up on the dawn cart to make the winter book square. Day 79 Shen Xiu signs clearance looking past her. Day 81 Sun He refuses the licence on the bounty standing and reads the pending filing in the same hour, and she signs the ceiling down on herself: **capped at Condensing 5, barred from Foundation without relicensing, her defect in her own hand, the sponsors' writ dead on the same page, Du Hong's living name in the book beside it.** Power: **Condensing 2.** Claim standing and unanswered, a paper about her body instead of her name, and a page in a file getting older every day.

**Ch 89–103 (Volume 2, batch 0008).** Pending life as attendance under pursuit: county hours, stamp days, tower readings, carrying work, buyer moving from records to bodies with a second man walking lanes. **A good coat makes a courtesy at the county door and she gives nothing back. The three desks are worked desk by desk and one clerk ends a thing he would rather not have been asked to do. The girl of twenty-four is on the page three times and says the true thing every time.** She builds four weeks of load and takes Condensing 3 at her own order, announced, paying with the market learning where she sleeps; arm settles at a third and stays. He hardens her own conclusion into a floor with the eleven as evidence and calls it the safe side; tower paper answers with *kiln-born* in a dead hand, second position on Lowwater's sheet, one of four copies, spoken once and unrecognised. Alone she works out being Chosen prices her — eleven spent at different rates, honours answering out of the taxed foundation — tells him, does not forgive. Hou's men stop Du Hong at the tower foot; he gives his own name, burns one sheet, refuses the place, is beaten into thaw water; she kills the second man with Nine-Step habit, nearly killed, no aperture-channel. Merit from the listed kill only, told-before-spent, yes, left-hand order gone, Settlement to ~11 weeks. He dies with Feng holding the lamp; proof fails; she buries him naming four causes, all hers. Power: **Condensing 3.** Claim standing unclaimed, pending standing capped, debt hers, cheap door gone. **The batch closes with the mechanism of the claim written down for the first time: the Watch had no form column for a layer act. And with her holding a second county document that is not about her advancement.**

**Ch 104–118 (Volume 2, batch 0009).** Grief and the grant. See the batch table above.

**Ch 119–132 (Volume 3, batch 0001).** Licensed life. She takes a hire on a board behind a well in Lowwater — six loads to a water channel at twelve coppers, paid by a stranger, and the first time in her life she has been worth a fixed small ordinary amount and wanted it. The licence's whole scope line is worth exactly that, and it costs her four loads a day for the county hour and a description she cannot get struck off her in a lane. He can see her arm go before she can, and tells her, and it costs nothing, and she can do nothing with it. A man with clean hands asks her **who certifies her hours** in a market and she answers truthfully, having four other moves and using none. The girl of twenty-four needs one true sentence about a licensed woman in a lane and gets it, and is not thanked, and the county's request for her schedule goes up the quarter on the girl's own hour. A relicensing turns out to be the same three legs again with an annex nobody has ever paid for: hours certified onto the Qinghe schedule are furnace hours **by certification and not by place**, and they unlevel a number that has been level since the spring. **She clears first, because three men will not put a mark on a sheet saying nine while her hours are in two places at once** — the order inverted from Volume 2 and the motive given to her by Deng Chao before anyone says the word because. Shen Xiu makes her write fifty-eight in her own hand and signs without looking up. The Hunter pays one coin for a certified copy of her schedule, reads the hole in her stage column, and says two true things in a county room in front of a queue: *the next stage must be announced or the paper fails its own terms*, and *a licensee capped at five cannot be examined past five*. **He is right about both and the registrar says so out loud while writing.** She holds the one weapon she will ever have — the sponsors' second book — in a coat for the whole volume and does not fire it, because they say *then don't*, and the seal is bought with silence, and what she can never get back is the not-knowing. Three marks go on a sheet; she writes **Condensing nine in her own hand** for the third time in the book and re-enters her own defect with a new line under it; and Sun He stamps it with seven conditions, of which the claim is the seventh and the last thing on the paper. Power: **Condensing 5, ceiling nine.** Nothing has moved but paper: the aperture is the same aperture, the arm is the same arm, the fourth stage is still on no paper in nine counties. Channel: **about three weeks.** She does not forgive him.

**Ch 133–148 (Volume 3, batch 0002).** Audit and mercy. Two clocks at once — the furnace floor and a lane nineteen minutes from the square — and the ceiling at nine turns out to be a permission and not a wage. The relicensing gave her a column, and the column has to be filled in a left hand, carried four miles to a stamp, every day; **the word *irregular* goes onto the front of the Qinghe furnace schedule** and nine men read it on the floor. She discovers the sentence she had walked past for four months — a licensee **may be taught past her stage** — and works out in eleven minutes that nobody in nine counties will put a name to it, and builds the stage herself out of load. **Condensing 6 announced at her own order** after six weeks and 240 loads; the announcement says *at Lowwater*, and there is one building in that town where a sentence becomes a door, and a Lowwater counting room opens its book again. A stranger asks a keeper for his work **in writing, lawfully, with six words in the margin**, and a keeper cannot lie: five sheets, the column at twenty-two years of five, **the four county copies walled behind the fifth item, one of them struck by an office no county keeps**, and her own hours in a fair hand at the back of a fair volume. A boy of seventeen with a finger finds the concealment in forty minutes. **Feng loses a quarter of his stair** — volumes, stipend, cough, house and column all stay, and *the district does not terminate an examination.* On the escort road a bounty man in good boots reads a boy of fourteen his own description out loud, and she kills him with the Nine-Step habit and one working arm and very nearly dies; **the tax takes the pace she spent five weeks building and cannot give it back, and the channel goes from about three weeks to about ten.** A Listener's appointment is offered with a coal-hook written on it in plain sight and **he takes it**: wage, oil, a hundredweight, a warm chair, and every hour she works upward truly for the rest of his life. She does not ask him not to. Relief that he is alive, immediately curdled. Then a guard's hire, a hired guard called Tan, a wheel she cannot see any more, four hours short at the season's close, and a **carriers' run list the girl of twenty-four has been copying for nobody for two months, which is the second hand on eleven days of a road.** The column comes out level at fifty-eight asked and fifty-eight worked. **Condensing 7 announced at her order from a mat in a loft**, and the fever takes the stair: **three counts, and it never comes back to two.** Power: **Condensing 7 of a ceiling of nine.** Nothing restored, nothing forgiven, and the eight hundred jin standing exactly where it stood.

**Ch 149–163 (Volume 3, batch 0003).** Hunting with a calendar. She breaks the one term she wrote in the spring **in public, on the copying floor, in front of a keeper who reports in full whether or not it is entered**, with the price named before the word — four and a half weeks out of about ten — and gets the four sheets of a handover **verified true, line by line, through her own eyes.** It does not buy the office name, the eleven, or design. The next morning she moves the table out of his room into the county office on the square, because a county writes down everything said in it. The offer is real: the voice certified as a misread natural echo, the designation drawn down painlessly over one winter, licence and stages standing, **and the eight hundred jin answered out of a purse that is not this county's, which pays the one man she owes and has never met.** And the fourth line of the second sheet says, in the ordinary place, that announcements already carried to the bound are not comprised in it. **She refuses aloud before two witnesses and signs it in her own hand**, and a clerk taking the agent's name off a form writes **Bai Zhu** into a Warden's book and into the county book, and she chose hunting over handing over with the whole thing verified true first. **She does not thank him and does not forgive him.** Then the volume turns into a diary: **the county examines her monthly instead of quarterly**, and the cost of a refusal is one morning in four and a copy on her member's sheet that goes to the Hall on the furnace schedule — to nine men who read their own schedule. A winter with no work in it; a quarry three miles east, a hoist worked by four men, eighteen coppers a load, and **a sledge handle that takes the heel of the hand she signs her hours with**, so that the county's in-person hour becomes a filing problem and she writes figures with the flat of a finger. A counting room that has entered a **date** against a standing claim, in a hand that is not the hand on the front. The boy of fourteen, who got in, is fifteen, owes four coppers and is paid three, and she says out loud in a carrier's yard that the description is about her and not about him, and pays for it with her own name in forty hands. **She suppresses stage eight at her own order in an empty hut at a quarry, and nothing goes out of her into nine counties at all** — and a suppressed stage leaves exactly the same absence as any other hole, the same width, and a man who was right at a public counter in June says so, politely, on a step, without asking her a single question, because she had verified a mercy instead of this. And on a shelf in the county office: **a request, numbered and dated, for an instrument not held in this county, after the reading of the winter return.** Nothing filed. A field man cannot unmake her inside a bound. Power: **Condensing 8, unannounced, on a column that reads one, two, three, five, six, seven, and then a space.** Channel **about three weeks** against eleven weeks of winter. Ledger is hers to order and his to hold only on her word. She does not forgive him.

**Ch 164–178 (Volume 3, batch 0004). Cost and signature — VOLUME 3 CLOSED.** The quarry runs out and takes the last paid work in the county with it: four days of stone in a face, five men where there were eleven, and a pay-off counted in the open at the rope in front of four other men, **two thousand and forty gross, six hundred and eighty of it gone at the hand, one thousand three hundred and sixty in her hand.** She keeps buying stone for a board behind a well at nine coppers a load when she has been paying five, and Da Shan tells her to her face that he has been charging her all along and is going to charge her more, and she works out that ninety-six coppers is the entire price of a thing nobody will ever read on paper. Then she finds out that the last private place she had was a room that was empty, works the cold end alone for the fifth week of five, and is told by the machinery of a clerk's notice that she has to stand in the one room she swore she would not stand in. **She goes.** **Qin, twenty-two, stands in the cold end for a shift and does not work, and she does not send him back** — she trades the last room that was hers by being empty for a witness, on purpose. She walks down four miles in the dark to have tomorrow entered **as a tower change, a thing that happened in the building and not a service rendered to a member**, and a keeper of sixty-two who has been warm for twenty-two years tells her to stand at the mark before the ninth hour, not at it, because nine men working is the same nine men and costs her nothing. **Then she says the word.** Condensing nine, at her own order, in the open, in front of nine men, and **it takes the space with it** — she is full top to the soles of her feet for the first time in her life — and it is over before the setting comes back out of the mark. **And she tells them, out loud, before they can work it out on their own over a fortnight, that a man is going to send a paper up past this county about what is in her head, and that there are fourteen days between the two.** That is the batch's irreversible step: nine counties are told exactly where she stands a fortnight before the referral goes up, and **the room that was hers by being empty fills with people and stays filled and will never be hers again.** Then she gives up the last private thing she had about her body, on purpose, to nine men on a furnace floor, and a hoisting man's whole response is *then say so at the frame and I will hold it*. At the third attendance **the county enters the nine and then the registrar discovers that her licence has nothing left on it** — the ceiling is nine, she is at nine, and there is nothing above nine on the document; above it is Foundation, and Foundation needs a second relicensing, and a second relicensing needs a schedule of hours worked at the ceiling asked for, **and this county does not keep a schedule of work done past its boundary.** The Hunter tells her one true thing without being asked and writes four words a day about a stage that was announced on the date she announced it. Then the winter return is read in order in a low room with a queue out into the square: **one, two, three, five, six, seven, nine**, three gaps ruled in the ordinary hand with four words, *Member present* at the foot, and the ruling said aloud and stopped because he has said it three times already. **She signs it herself, eleven minutes with the flat of a second finger, in front of a queue that does not shuffle.** And then a patient man who has never lied to her **certifies in a Warden's hand that the thing in her head is real, unauthorised, and carried by a licensee in good standing, and refers the dismantling upward to the Warden for want of a Wall writ** — the instrument he does not hold, the one thing in eleven weeks he has been buying. The referral carries her name, her stages in order and her hours, and carries no agent's name, and goes up the stair past every office she has ever stood in front of. **The subject is standing in the room, and it is hers at ch 150 that put her there.** The next day she files the only document that can be answered, on the relicensed paper, with her own licence folded in behind it so that the eighth hundred jin goes up the stair with it — and it is **received, numbered, routed and barred, not refused**, on the ground that this county cannot certify a schedule of work past its boundary, with a last line written slower than the rest: *the answer to this application is not this office's to write.* **Once entered it cannot be withdrawn.** Everything that can leave this county leaves it in one crate. The district answers the Hunter's request the way a district answers. Nine men read the schedule and Gao does the sum out loud in a hall. She buys four nails, takes the first licence down off two nails before it goes soft, and practises: three straight out of five in the dark, then a whole morning in the sun, where the second hour teaches her that the hand does not want to be watched. And in the first week of the deep cold, with ten people asleep in twelve bunks and one light burning four miles off in a tower whose stair is carried this quarter by a clerk who comes down empty, **the second licence goes up straight above the second bunk**, because she has two arms' worth of practice at nailing and one arm. She asks how much of it is there and he tells her the truth: **about ten days**, against about six weeks of winter still to come in it. She does the arithmetic against a calendar with a barred petition in it and nothing else. She has the Ledger to order and she does not order it. **She does not forgive him. She does not tell him to stop.**

**Ch 179–192 (Volume 4, batch 0001).** Ration and silence. A month with a date at the end of it: the county publishes the day the season's road reopens, a clerk has sealed a date onto a boundary, and the district's answer to the Hunter's request is still **four words and an empty space the width of a line that nobody in this county can write in.** The tin dies at the end of the month, seven days short of the road, and she does not tell nine men. In the only warm room in four miles she finds she wanted Feng to ask her a question and he will not, because a keeper who asks has begun to want something. She buys a name for **eight days out of about ten**, saying the price out loud before the word, and gets **Luo Ping** — a schedule-man of thirty-nine with a wife and a boy past this county's boundary — and finds out that a name is not a ledger entry. She rules him to four sentences a day on the back of a dead quarry weight ticket, he corrects her once and never again, and the ration thins on its own because she has been spending him on questions she already knows the answer to. **She refuses the Ledger out loud at the easiest moment in the batch** and buys the shape of the road out of paper instead, and the run lists show that the carriers do not run past a certain point because there is nothing on the far side of it for them to run to. She stops buying the stone in daylight at a board behind a well and makes nine men cold for four nights to hold thirty coppers for a road, and a man of twenty-two fills a bucket himself and asks nothing. And the year of non-forgiveness that has run since ch 100 **changes shape without closing**: the sentence she kept back once a year to say out loud goes on a price instead, and what replaces the refusal is not forgiveness and not absolution — it is a purchase, and relief at not having to prove she is angry to an empty room, and the cost of it, which is that she has agreed to spend him. Power: **Condensing 9 throughout; not one bracketed line in fourteen chapters.** Foundation 1 is ch 207, in Batch 0002, and the road opens on day 254 with her licence at a ceiling of nine and nothing above it anywhere in the world.

## Volume 6 — The Real Realm (ch 299–360)

**`outline/volume-06.md` is the authority.** Batch 0001 drafted against it and against the fifteen cards at
`outline/batches/volume-06-batch-0001.md`. Full record: `state/continuity.md`, *Established facts, Volume 6
Batch 0001 (ch 299–313) — READING AND BEING READ* and *Volume 6 running ledger — at end of ch 313*.

### Batch 0001 (ch 299–313) — reading and being read. WRITTEN, 24,529 words over fifteen chapters, day 621 to day 645, average ~1,635. ONE bracketed layer line, at ch 310.

- **Ch 299 — *The First Cart* (day 621, the eighth).** She walks two miles east for the first time in five months, past a wheel she has stood half a mile short of since the night of the seventh. The mill is a working building and the wheel is not what the place is for. Four books go into four trays, and the cord is tied by a man who does not look at what is in it. The cart out is not the cart in; it belongs to a round and it goes on and she is not going with it. **She wanted the box handed to her at the loading door and there was no version of that morning in which it was.** She sleeps on the boards in the shed and does not go back. *Emotion: she expected to be doing something and she is watching a man tie a cord.*

- **Ch 300 — *The Closed Season* (days 622–624, the ninth to the eleventh).** Two days of unpaid, unbooked lifting in a shed, because a man who sorts trays all day will let a woman lift one if she lifts it right, and that is neither an employment nor a kindness. On the eleventh at the second hour she asks one working question — *what goes up* — and gets **the rule of the circuit in five sentences**: a book that shuts clean on the thirtieth is a finished book, it goes in a tray, the tray goes up on the mill road on the first cart, every reach on that ground sends one a month, up the road past the store there is a stair in the counting house and everything that goes up it is read against everything else that went up it, and **nothing comes back down.** She does not ask a second question. She works out from a season that closed on the thirtieth and a mill the books are taken to that hers went up too. He offers her the floor till the cart wants turning back. *Emotion: the relief of a rule — the first simple thing since the crossing, distrusted immediately and used anyway.*

- **Ch 301 — *What The Counter Said* (days 625–626, the twelfth and the thirteenth).** Two miles and four hundred yards west at the second hour. The seasonal book has been out of that room for the first time in five months and the shape it left has to be accounted for; the clerk writes a line with a date in it and **the rule at the side of the page, which is the same rule on every reach and which he did not write.** No name on the line. She does not ask him to fetch the tray and does not ask him to write her name anywhere, and the not-asking costs her something. On the thirteenth she goes back because she had not finished looking, and finds a shelf holding a sheet of paper weighted under a bit of iron, and leaves. *Emotion: she is not the subject of anything that happened today, and she is the only reader of a fact about herself.*

- **Ch 302 — *Past The Last Farm* (days 627–628, the fourteenth and the fifteenth).** There is no hiring on that ground and never has been. **A gate, a day's work, and a rate the farmer says out loud once: seven coppers a reach.** No stub, no name, no booking, no term, and pay in her own hand at the end of the day. She clears the first reach herself at the sixth hour in front of a man who is not watching her, with four fingers, an arm at half and four counts of breath, and nothing she has lost stops her. **Wei Ran goes past with a team and does not stop the cart** — a rate, a road about what the ground does to a wheel in the wet, and an opinion, in about as many sentences as it takes to say them, and no offer of anything. *Emotion: ashamed of how fast she walked a mile to find out what a reach of drain cost.*

- **Ch 303 — *Seven Coppers At The Gate* (day 629, the sixteenth).** **Eight coppers** for a reach and a little over, into her own hand. Bread, then material at a lean-to past the mill, then what is left, and she does not do the sum twice. The oilcloth comes out against her ribs: **eighteen counterfoils in four marks, no plank, no nineteenth that any living thing on this side of nine days of road can produce.** On the flat stone by the door at the dark, eleven minutes of the flat of a second finger to break a poor piece of clay to a size one arm can carry, and the ring relit banked low out of what is left. She does not say out loud what the ground under it does. *Emotion: relief that is not relief — a thing which answers her is a thing she can be found at.*

- **Ch 304 — *What The Box Was For* (day 630, the seventeenth) — MIDPOINT.** Four ordinary days — a number, a road, a light, a drain — with a tray going somewhere she cannot follow sitting under all of them. On the seventeenth she puts two facts together in the ordinary voice in a drain bank in about four seconds: every book that closes clean on the thirtieth goes up a stair to a floor where somebody reads it; hers closed on the thirtieth and went up on the first cart; there is one name in it and a word above the hours and she put the word there; **so somebody has read her. Not my hours. Me.** The turn: **she wanted to be checked, she has been checked, and a reader is not a witness** — a reader knows what it says and can act on it and does not have to be near it. The seventeenth comes and nothing happens on it, in one sentence. *Emotion, under everything and worse: she would not have given the stage back, and would have gone into it again knowing the stair.*
